### Exploring Position Encoding in Diffusion U-Net for Training-free High-resolution Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09830v1)
- **Authors**: Feng Zhou, Pu Cao, Yiyang Ma, Lu Yang, Jianqin Yin
- **Abstract**: Denoising higher-resolution latents via a pre-trained U-Net leads to repetitive and disordered image patterns. Although recent studies make efforts to improve generative quality by aligning denoising process across original and higher resolutions, the root cause of suboptimal generation is still lacking exploration. Through comprehensive analysis of position encoding in U-Net, we attribute it to inconsistent position encoding, sourced by the inadequate propagation of position information from zero-padding to latent features in convolution layers as resolution increases. To address this issue, we propose a novel training-free approach, introducing a Progressive Boundary Complement (PBC) method. This method creates dynamic virtual image boundaries inside the feature map to enhance position information propagation, enabling high-quality and rich-content high-resolution image synthesis. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of our method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of repetitive and disordered patterns when generating high-resolution images using pre-trained diffusion U-Nets. The authors attribute this issue to inconsistent position encoding within the U-Net, specifically caused by inadequate propagation of position information from zero-padding, especially as the feature map resolution increases. To counter this, they propose a novel, training-free approach called Progressive Boundary Complement (PBC).  PBC introduces dynamic virtual image boundaries inside the feature map to improve position information propagation, leading to high-quality, content-rich, high-resolution image synthesis. They demonstrate the effectiveness of their method through extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Identification:**  The paper effectively identifies and articulates a specific problem in high-resolution image generation with diffusion models: inconsistent position encoding stemming from zero-padding. This is a valuable contribution, as many existing solutions take an engineering-driven approach without fully addressing the underlying cause.
    *   **Technical Novelty:** The proposed PBC method is conceptually novel and technically sound. The idea of creating virtual boundaries and using them to facilitate position information propagation is well-explained and implemented.
    *   **Training-Free:** The fact that PBC is a training-free method is a significant advantage. This avoids the computational cost and potential data bias associated with fine-tuning or retraining.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments, including quantitative metrics (KID, IS, HPS, ImageReward, Content Richness) and qualitative comparisons, to demonstrate the effectiveness of PBC. The introduction of the 'Content Richness' metric attempts to address a gap in existing evaluation measures and, while not perfect, contributes to the evaluation process.
    *   **Content Richness is Good:** The virtual boundaries not only fix position encoding but also contribute to content.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** While the paper identifies the problem of position encoding, the theoretical analysis remains somewhat superficial.  A deeper dive into the mathematical properties of position information propagation in convolutional layers could strengthen the argument.
    *   **Content Richness Metric:** While the metric makes an attempt at addressing the gap in evaluation metrics, it does not address the drawbacks or limitations. The metric seems to have a high level of abstraction.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** The experiments are conducted on SD-XL. While this is a common and powerful model, the generalizability of PBC to other diffusion architectures (e.g., models with transformer backbones) is not explored.
    *   **Presentation Issues:** The writing style is slightly verbose and could be more concise.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper provides a valuable insight into the underlying cause of generation failures in high-resolution generative models, which has not been extensively explored in previous research.
    *   The PBC method itself presents a fresh approach to address the positional encoding problem, and it achieves impressive results without the need for training.
    *   The paper's findings can potentially inspire new research directions in diffusion model design and training strategies.

*   **Justification for Score:**
    This paper is of high quality overall. It addresses a significant problem in the field of high-resolution image generation, introduces a novel training-free approach (PBC) with solid experimental validation and also proposes a content richness metric. However, the theoretical analysis could be more rigorous, and the generalizability of the findings needs further investigation. Given these considerations, a rigorous evaluation leads to the following score:

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Media and responsible AI governance: a game-theoretic and LLM analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09858v1)
- **Authors**: Nataliya Balabanova, Adeela Bashir, Paolo Bova, Alessio Buscemi, Theodor Cimpeanu, Henrique Correia da Fonseca, Alessandro Di Stefano, Manh Hong Duong, Elias Fernandez Domingos, Antonio Fernandes, The Anh Han, Marcus Krellner, Ndidi Bianca Ogbo, Simon T. Powers, Daniele Proverbio, Fernando P. Santos, Zia Ush Shamszaman, Zhao Song
- **Abstract**: This paper investigates the complex interplay between AI developers, regulators, users, and the media in fostering trustworthy AI systems. Using evolutionary game theory and large language models (LLMs), we model the strategic interactions among these actors under different regulatory regimes. The research explores two key mechanisms for achieving responsible governance, safe AI development and adoption of safe AI: incentivising effective regulation through media reporting, and conditioning user trust on commentariats' recommendation. The findings highlight the crucial role of the media in providing information to users, potentially acting as a form of "soft" regulation by investigating developers or regulators, as a substitute to institutional AI regulation (which is still absent in many regions). Both game-theoretic analysis and LLM-based simulations reveal conditions under which effective regulation and trustworthy AI development emerge, emphasising the importance of considering the influence of different regulatory regimes from an evolutionary game-theoretic perspective. The study concludes that effective governance requires managing incentives and costs for high quality commentaries.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the complex interplay between AI developers, regulators, users, and the media in fostering trustworthy AI systems. It uses evolutionary game theory (EGT) and large language models (LLMs) to model strategic interactions among these actors under different regulatory regimes. The research focuses on the mechanisms of media reporting and user trust, to explore responsible governance and safe AI development. The paper examines the impact of media in providing information to users, acting as a "soft" regulation and complementing institutional AI regulation. Both game-theoretic analysis and LLM simulations are used to identify conditions where effective regulation and trustworthy AI development emerge.  The study concludes that effective governance requires managing incentives and costs for high-quality media commentaries.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Timeliness and Relevance:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and timely topic: AI governance. With the rapid development of AI, understanding how to incentivize responsible development and use is crucial.
*   **Methodological Rigor:**  The paper combines EGT and LLM-based simulations, offering both theoretical insights and empirical explorations. Using EGT provides a structured framework for analyzing strategic interactions, while LLMs offer the potential to simulate human-like behavior within these frameworks.
*   **Novelty:** The paper is novel in that it uses EGT to model media influence within AI governance, explicitly considering the media as a self-interested agent. Prior work often treats these actors as more simplistic components of the regulatory system.  The incorporation of LLMs to test the EGT findings is also relatively novel.
*   **Potential Impact:** The findings regarding media as a form of "soft" regulation could be influential in shaping real-world policy debates on AI governance. Highlighting the importance of institutional transparency in encouraging safe AI development can be a very positive impact on the field.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Model Simplifications:** As with any EGT model, the paper relies on simplifications of complex real-world interactions. The model might omit important factors influencing the behavior of the actors, such as political considerations, lobbying activities, or ethical values.
*   **LLM Limitations:** The use of LLMs is interesting, but there are inherent limitations. LLMs are not perfect replicas of human behavior, and their responses can be sensitive to prompting strategies and model biases. It is difficult to validate the use of LLMs as a proxy for real-world actors.
*   **Generalizability:**  The specific parameter choices within the model can influence the results. While the paper explores a range of parameters, it is not clear how generalizable the findings are to different AI contexts or regulatory environments. Specific contexts, such as healthcare AI vs. autonomous weapons, would require different parameters.
*   **Limited Empirical Validation:** While the LLM simulations provide some validation, the paper lacks real-world empirical data to support its theoretical claims. Policy recommendations should ideally be grounded in more extensive empirical studies.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by incorporating media influence into the AI governance framework. The combination of EGT and LLM methods is compelling, and the theoretical insights are potentially impactful. However, the inherent limitations of the modeling approach and lack of thorough empirical validation limit its overall impact. The paper also seems to build off previous works by the same authors, which decreases the originality. Therefore, the paper is a good, but not exceptional, contribution.

### Leveraging Semantic Attribute Binding for Free-Lunch Color Control in Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09864v1)
- **Authors**: Héctor Laria, Alexandra Gomez-Villa, Jiang Qin, Muhammad Atif Butt, Bogdan Raducanu, Javier Vazquez-Corral, Joost van de Weijer, Kai Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models have enabled remarkable control over various attributes, yet precise color specification remains a fundamental challenge. Existing approaches, such as ColorPeel, rely on model personalization, requiring additional optimization and limiting flexibility in specifying arbitrary colors. In this work, we introduce ColorWave, a novel training-free approach that achieves exact RGB-level color control in diffusion models without fine-tuning. By systematically analyzing the cross-attention mechanisms within IP-Adapter, we uncover an implicit binding between textual color descriptors and reference image features. Leveraging this insight, our method rewires these bindings to enforce precise color attribution while preserving the generative capabilities of pretrained models. Our approach maintains generation quality and diversity, outperforming prior methods in accuracy and applicability across diverse object categories. Through extensive evaluations, we demonstrate that ColorWave establishes a new paradigm for structured, color-consistent diffusion-based image synthesis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ColorWave, a novel training-free approach for precise color control in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. ColorWave leverages the inherent semantic attribute binding within the IP-Adapter framework to achieve accurate RGB-level color specification without requiring fine-tuning. By analyzing the cross-attention mechanisms in IP-Adapter, the authors discovered an implicit connection between textual color descriptors and reference image features. ColorWave rewires these connections to enforce precise color attribution while preserving the generative capabilities of pre-trained models. The approach includes automatic color name generation and spatial prior addition to ensure accurate color placement.  The authors demonstrate superior performance in color accuracy and generation quality compared to existing methods, particularly ColorPeel, across various object categories.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the discovery and exploitation of the "semantic attribute binding" phenomenon within IP-Adapter. While IP-Adapter itself is not new, the authors are the first to identify and leverage this latent capability for precise color control. The technique of "rewiring" cross-attention to enhance color attribution is also novel. The training-free nature of the approach is a significant advantage over methods requiring per-color optimization.

*   **Significance:** Achieving precise color control in T2I models is a fundamental challenge with numerous applications. ColorWave addresses this challenge effectively without the need for expensive and limiting fine-tuning. This opens doors for more flexible and practical design workflows. If widely adopted, this approach has the potential to significantly impact how designers interact with T2I systems for color-critical tasks. The identified latent space binding, if proven generalizable to other domains or network architectures, offers a new avenue for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-Free:** Eliminates the need for per-color optimization, drastically improving usability and efficiency.
    *   **High Accuracy:**  Demonstrated superior color accuracy compared to other training-free methods and competitive performance against fine-tuned models.
    *   **Generality:** Works across diverse object categories without specialized training.
    *   **Well-Explained Methodology:**  Clear description of the underlying mechanisms and the proposed approach.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:**  Comprehensive qualitative and quantitative evaluations with appropriate metrics and comparisons to relevant baselines.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on IP-Adapter:** ColorWave's performance depends on the underlying quality and capabilities of the IP-Adapter framework.
    *   **Limited Multi-Object Color Control:** The method currently struggles with complex scenes requiring distinct colors for multiple objects.  The cross-attention degradation and color bleeding issues pose limitations.
    *   **Semantic Proximity Effect:**  Color attribution effectiveness is influenced by the perceptual and semantic distance between prompt color and target color, indicating a dependency on pretrained color knowledge.
    *   While the paper highlights the benefits of being training-free, the reliance on IP-Adapter requires it to be pretrained on an image dataset. The semantic attribute binding is happening at the training stage of IP-Adapter rather than at inference time.

*   **Potential Impact:** The research offers a significant advancement in color control for diffusion models. The training-free aspect and general applicability could make it a valuable tool for designers, artists, and other users who require precise color manipulation. The identified attribute binding, if exploited across domains, could lead to new controllable generative techniques.

*   **Critique:** While the paper presents a strong contribution, some aspects limit its overall impact and novelty. The dependence on the IP-Adapter architecture somewhat constrains its potential adoption in other contexts. The observed "semantic proximity effect" indicates an implicit bias from the pre-trained model's color knowledge. The multi-object limitations also affect its real-world applicability.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

ColorWave's training-free, precise color control offers a significant improvement over existing methods. The discovery of semantic attribute binding in IP-Adapter and its clever exploitation are both innovative and insightful. While the IP-Adapter dependency, semantic limitations, and struggles with multi-object color specifications temper the overall significance, the paper introduces an important new approach and sets the stage for future research. If these limitations are addressed in subsequent work, the potential impact of this research is substantial.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### LuciBot: Automated Robot Policy Learning from Generated Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09871v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaowen Qiu, Yian Wang, Jiting Cai, Zhehuan Chen, Chunru Lin, Tsun-Hsuan Wang, Chuang Gan
- **Abstract**: Automatically generating training supervision for embodied tasks is crucial, as manual designing is tedious and not scalable. While prior works use large language models (LLMs) or vision-language models (VLMs) to generate rewards, these approaches are largely limited to simple tasks with well-defined rewards, such as pick-and-place. This limitation arises because LLMs struggle to interpret complex scenes compressed into text or code due to their restricted input modality, while VLM-based rewards, though better at visual perception, remain limited by their less expressive output modality. To address these challenges, we leverage the imagination capability of general-purpose video generation models. Given an initial simulation frame and a textual task description, the video generation model produces a video demonstrating task completion with correct semantics. We then extract rich supervisory signals from the generated video, including 6D object pose sequences, 2D segmentations, and estimated depth, to facilitate task learning in simulation. Our approach significantly improves supervision quality for complex embodied tasks, enabling large-scale training in simulators.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LuciBot, a framework for automated robot policy learning using video generation models.  The core idea is to leverage the imagination capability of general-purpose video generation models to produce videos demonstrating task completion. These videos, conditioned on an initial simulation frame and a textual task description, are then used to extract rich supervisory signals (6D object poses, segmentations, depth) to train robot policies in simulation. This approach aims to overcome limitations of existing methods that rely on LLMs or VLMs with restricted input/output modalities or domain-specific fine-tuning. LuciBot is evaluated on a diverse set of embodied tasks and claims improved performance over various baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using video generation models for robot learning supervision is interesting and potentially novel. Existing work has used video prediction and/or video generation for various robot learning tasks, but using a general-purpose, *pre-trained* video generation model to derive diverse supervision signals is a relatively fresh approach. The novelty lies in the specific combination of leveraging pre-trained video generation models *without* domain-specific finetuning, extracting rich supervision signals like poses/segmentations, and employing them for policy learning in a complex embodied environment. However, there are some prior works in similar spirit using video generation models as reward functions or policies. The novelty is incremental rather than breakthrough.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper hinges on whether it truly unlocks complex embodied tasks that are beyond the reach of existing methods. The paper demonstrates improved performance on a diverse suite of tasks including deformable objects, fluid dynamics and articulated objects. However, the tasks are still constrained to simulation and the complexity, while improved, isn't fundamentally breaking new ground for complex manipulations. A crucial aspect is the *generalizability* of the approach to unseen tasks and real-world scenarios without task-specific fine-tuning. The paper includes some real-world evaluations which strengthens the contribution but are preliminary.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a crucial challenge in embodied AI: automated supervision generation.
    *   Leverages the power of recent video generation models without expensive fine-tuning.
    *   Extracts diverse and informative supervision signals beyond simple rewards.
    *   Demonstrates improved performance on a diverse set of tasks.
    *   Attempts real-world transfer, a critical step in validating the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on video generation models means that the quality of supervision is limited by the quality and consistency of generated videos. The paper implements various methods to mitigate it.
    *   The approach may be limited to tasks with relatively short horizons due to accumulated errors in longer videos.
    *   The policy is optimized in simulation, requiring a digital twin of real world scenario to make the transfer.
    *   The evaluations are constrained to simulation, limiting its practical impact.
    *   Although a diverse suite of tasks is considered, the improvement is marginal in certain cases, therefore, the significance of the paper is reduced.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty and significance mentioned above and taking into account its strengths and weaknesses, a score of 6/10 seems appropriate. This reflects that the work is interesting with a good engineering contribution (combining a few different techniques), demonstrates solid improvements over baselines, but has potential limitations in video generation, task complexity, and real-world applicability. Its novelty isn't groundbreaking, but it builds upon and extends existing approaches in a useful way.  It is more than an average paper (5) since there is a clear technical contribution. I can see that this paper can influence other researches in automated training dataset generation.

### What's In Your Field? Mapping Scientific Research with Knowledge Graphs and Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09894v1)
- **Authors**: Abhipsha Das, Nicholas Lourie, Siavash Golkar, Mariel Pettee
- **Abstract**: The scientific literature's exponential growth makes it increasingly challenging to navigate and synthesize knowledge across disciplines. Large language models (LLMs) are powerful tools for understanding scientific text, but they fail to capture detailed relationships across large bodies of work. Unstructured approaches, like retrieval augmented generation, can sift through such corpora to recall relevant facts; however, when millions of facts influence the answer, unstructured approaches become cost prohibitive. Structured representations offer a natural complement -- enabling systematic analysis across the whole corpus. Recent work enhances LLMs with unstructured or semistructured representations of scientific concepts; to complement this, we try extracting structured representations using LLMs. By combining LLMs' semantic understanding with a schema of scientific concepts, we prototype a system that answers precise questions about the literature as a whole. Our schema applies across scientific fields and we extract concepts from it using only 20 manually annotated abstracts. To demonstrate the system, we extract concepts from 30,000 papers on arXiv spanning astrophysics, fluid dynamics, and evolutionary biology. The resulting database highlights emerging trends and, by visualizing the knowledge graph, offers new ways to explore the ever-growing landscape of scientific knowledge. Demo: abby101/surveyor-0 on HF Spaces. Code: https://github.com/chiral-carbon/kg-for-science.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary, a rigorous critical evaluation, and a justified novelty/significance score for the provided research paper, focusing on being critical in the assessment.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a system that combines large language models (LLMs) with structured knowledge graphs to map and analyze scientific research literature. The system extracts categorized concepts from scientific papers using a schema covering research entities like objects, datasets, methods, and modalities.  The authors use a few-shot learning approach with LLama-3 to populate a SQL database with these concepts extracted from ~30,000 papers across astrophysics, fluid dynamics, and evolutionary biology. A force-directed graph visualization and an interactive query interface enable researchers to explore concept relationships, track trends, and gain insights into methodological patterns across domains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles the increasing difficulty of navigating and synthesizing knowledge in the face of exponentially growing scientific literature. The approach of combining LLMs with structured data is well-motivated.
    *   **Domain-Agnostic Schema:** The generic schema for categorizing scientific concepts across different fields is a notable strength. Creating a schema that works for multiple domains is challenging and, if successful, promotes wider applicability.
    *   **Interactive System:** The interactive query interface and visualization offer a user-friendly way to explore the extracted knowledge, which increases the utility of the system for researchers.
    *   **Practical Demonstration:** The authors demonstrate their system by extracting concepts from a reasonable-sized corpus of papers and showcasing examples of how it can be used to answer research questions. This helps to demonstrate the utility of their approach.
    *   **Scalable LLM-based Extraction Pipeline:** They use a scalable LLM extraction pipeline, which is a valuable contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Extraction Accuracy:** The precision and recall scores reported (44% and 31%, respectively) are relatively low, which raises concerns about the reliability of the extracted knowledge. The quality of the knowledge graph heavily depends on the accuracy of the initial extraction.
    *   **Limited Manual Annotation:** Using only 20 manually annotated abstracts as training data for few-shot learning might be insufficient to achieve robust performance. Increasing the size and diversity of the annotated set would likely improve the extraction accuracy.
    *   **Over-reliance on LLMs:** The method heavily relies on the LLM's ability to understand and categorize scientific concepts correctly. This approach could be sensitive to the LLM's biases and limitations, and alternative approaches with a higher degree of constraint might be more reliable.
    *   **Lack of Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper lacks a rigorous evaluation against baseline methods (e.g., keyword extraction, RAG). While the examples provided are informative, a quantitative comparison would strengthen the paper's claims.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The overall approach of combining LLMs with structured data and visualization is not entirely novel. Several existing systems explore similar ideas. The paper's novelty lies primarily in the specific schema and the domain-agnostic implementation.

*   **Significance:**

    *   If the extraction accuracy could be improved, the system could potentially be valuable for researchers seeking to identify trends, track the evolution of scientific concepts, and explore connections between different fields.
    *   The domain-agnostic schema could serve as a useful starting point for developing similar systems in other domains.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a system that addresses an important problem and offers a practical approach for exploring scientific literature. However, the relatively low extraction accuracy, the lack of rigorous evaluation against baselines, and incremental novelty limit the paper's significance. While the domain-agnostic schema is a positive aspect, the overall performance of the system needs to be substantially improved to achieve a high impact.

### Improving the Reusability of Conversational Search Test Collections
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09899v1)
- **Authors**: Zahra Abbasiantaeb, Chuan Meng, Leif Azzopardi, Mohammad Aliannejadi
- **Abstract**: Incomplete relevance judgments limit the reusability of test collections. When new systems are compared to previous systems that contributed to the pool, they often face a disadvantage. This is due to pockets of unjudged documents (called holes) in the test collection that the new systems return. The very nature of Conversational Search (CS) means that these holes are potentially larger and more problematic when evaluating systems. In this paper, we aim to extend CS test collections by employing Large Language Models (LLMs) to fill holes by leveraging existing judgments. We explore this problem using TREC iKAT 23 and TREC CAsT 22 collections, where information needs are highly dynamic and the responses are much more varied, leaving bigger holes to fill. Our experiments reveal that CS collections show a trend towards less reusability in deeper turns. Also, fine-tuning the Llama 3.1 model leads to high agreement with human assessors, while few-shot prompting the ChatGPT results in low agreement with humans. Consequently, filling the holes of a new system using ChatGPT leads to a higher change in the location of the new system. While regenerating the assessment pool with few-shot prompting the ChatGPT model and using it for evaluation achieves a high rank correlation with human-assessed pools. We show that filling the holes using few-shot training the Llama 3.1 model enables a fairer comparison between the new system and the systems contributed to the pool. Our hole-filling model based on few-shot training of the Llama 3.1 model can improve the reusability of test collections.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of reusability in conversational search (CS) test collections, which is hampered by incomplete relevance judgments ("holes"). The authors propose using Large Language Models (LLMs) to fill these holes by leveraging existing judgments. They experiment with TREC iKAT 23 and TREC CAST 22 datasets, demonstrating that reusability declines with deeper turns in conversations. The paper explores using both commercial (GPT-3.5) and open-source (Llama-3.1) LLMs in zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuning settings for relevance judgment. They find that fine-tuning Llama-3.1 achieves higher agreement with human assessors than few-shot prompting GPT-3.5. Furthermore, filling holes with judgments from fine-tuned Llama-3.1 enables a fairer comparison between new and existing systems in the pool. The authors show improved reusability when employing Llama-3.1 for hole-filling using the few-shot approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a valuable contribution by investigating the use of LLMs for improving the reusability of CS test collections. While LLMs have been explored for relevance judgment in ad-hoc search, their application to the more complex CS setting and the focus on addressing the "hole-filling" problem are relatively novel. The exploration of both commercial and open-source LLMs adds to the breadth of the research.
*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is significant because the reusability of test collections directly impacts the cost and fairness of evaluating new systems. CS poses a particularly difficult challenge due to the dynamic nature of information needs and the diversity of system responses, leading to larger "holes." The paper offers a practical solution to improve the comparability of systems, which has direct implications for future research and development in CS. The findings concerning Llama-3.1's superior agreement with human assessors in the fine-tuning setting are also important for choosing the appropriate LLM for this task.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined research questions and clear methodology.
    *   Comprehensive experiments with two different CS datasets and multiple LLM configurations.
    *   Rigorous evaluation using various metrics, including agreement with human labels, rank correlation, and rank change.
    *   The comparison of GPT-3.5 and Llama-3.1 offers insights into the trade-offs between commercial and open-source LLMs for relevance judgment.
    *   The paper clearly articulates the limitations of the study and identifies future research directions.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the improvements are valuable, they are incremental.
    *   The gains achieved through the proposed approach, while positive, might not be substantial enough to warrant a complete overhaul of existing evaluation practices. The extent to which improvements are evident over existing baselines or other pooling methods is not entirely made clear.
    *   The reliance on a specific version of Llama-3.1 may limit the reproducibility of the results.
    *   The transferability of the findings to other CS datasets or LLMs is not fully established. The experiments focus on a specific set of conditions, and it remains unclear how well the proposed approach would generalize.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field by addressing a specific and important problem in the evaluation of conversational search systems. While the approach has merit and the experiments are well-conducted, the overall novelty is incremental, and the improvements, although positive, could be more significant to make a very substantial change to current methodologies. The comparative analysis is comprehensive, including the comparisons with human annotations and existing systems. The paper, however, can be strengthened by comparing their hole-filling strategies to baselines like judgement aggregation (e.g. [REF]). Given the limitations of the study and the existing literature, a score of 7 seems appropriate.

### Conversational Gold: Evaluating Personalized Conversational Search System using Gold Nuggets
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09902v1)
- **Authors**: Zahra Abbasiantaeb, Simon Lupart, Leif Azzopardi, Jeffery Dalton, Mohammad Aliannejadi
- **Abstract**: The rise of personalized conversational search systems has been driven by advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), enabling these systems to retrieve and generate answers for complex information needs. However, the automatic evaluation of responses generated by Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) systems remains an understudied challenge. In this paper, we introduce a new resource for assessing the retrieval effectiveness and relevance of response generated by RAG systems, using a nugget-based evaluation framework. Built upon the foundation of TREC iKAT 2023, our dataset extends to the TREC iKAT 2024 collection, which includes 17 conversations and 20,575 relevance passage assessments, together with 2,279 extracted gold nuggets, and 62 manually written gold answers from NIST assessors. While maintaining the core structure of its predecessor, this new collection enables a deeper exploration of generation tasks in conversational settings. Key improvements in iKAT 2024 include: (1) ``gold nuggets'' -- concise, essential pieces of information extracted from relevant passages of the collection -- which serve as a foundation for automatic response evaluation; (2) manually written answers to provide a gold standard for response evaluation; (3) unanswerable questions to evaluate model hallucination; (4) expanded user personas, providing richer contextual grounding; and (5) a transition from Personal Text Knowledge Base (PTKB) ranking to PTKB classification and selection. Built on this resource, we provide a framework for long-form answer generation evaluation, involving nuggets extraction and nuggets matching, linked to retrieval. This establishes a solid resource for advancing research in personalized conversational search and long-form answer generation. Our resources are publicly available at https://github.com/irlabamsterdam/CONE-RAG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new resource, iKAT 2024, designed to evaluate personalized conversational search systems using a nugget-based framework. It builds upon the TREC IKAT 2023 collection, extending it with more conversations, relevance passage assessments, extracted gold nuggets, and manually written gold answers. Key improvements include the "gold nuggets" for automatic response evaluation, manually written answers for response evaluation, unanswerable questions to evaluate model hallucination, expanded user personas, and a shift from PTKB ranking to classification and selection. The paper also provides a framework, CONE-RAG, for long-form answer generation evaluation involving nugget extraction and matching linked to retrieval. The resources are publicly available. The experiments within the paper compare the performance of LLM-extracted nuggets with human-extracted nuggets, and the usefulness of different methods for matching responses and nuggets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper does present a novel resource, iKAT 2024, which extends an existing test collection with significant enhancements, particularly the inclusion of human-annotated gold nuggets. The shift from ranking to classification for PTKB is also a valuable adjustment. The CONE-RAG framework, while not conceptually groundbreaking (nugget-based evaluation has been around), offers a concrete implementation tailored for personalized conversational search. However, nugget-based evaluation for summarization is not new, and the overall approach relies on well-established techniques. The reliance on LLMs for parts of the process (even if it's comparing them to human performance) introduces potential biases.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the challenges of evaluating RAG systems in the context of *personalized* conversational search.  The emphasis on personalizing CSA is important, given it’s one the main difference between conversation search with a traditional one. The new test collection can provide a strong test bench for evaluating the system with different users/personas. By publicly releasing the resources, the paper facilitates further research and development in this area. The analysis of LLM-based vs. human-based nugget extraction provides practical insights for other researchers. However, the extent to which this resource will be adopted and actually move the field forward remains to be seen. A crucial element for widespread adoption will be how difficult it is for research groups to interact with and reuse.

* **Strengths:**
    * Publicly available, well-documented resource
    * Address an important and under-studied problem: personalized CSA evaluation
    * Inclusion of negative examples (unanswerable questions)
    * Thorough data collection process (NIST assessors)
    * Detailed analysis of LLM performance in nugget extraction and matching.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Potential LLM bias in the evaluation pipeline. LLMs are used in various part of the framework and introduce an uncertainty on the final evaluation
    * The reliance on specific LLMs (GPT-40, DeBERTa) may limit generalizability.
    * Lack of compelling novel evaluation metrics.  While CONE-RAG is a useful framework, the core metrics (precision, recall) remain standard.
    * Limited discussion on the limitations of the nugget-based approach itself.
    * Limited new insights from experimental results given well-established methods.

* **Potential Influence:** The iKAT 2024 collection and CONE-RAG could become valuable benchmarks for evaluating personalized conversational search systems, especially RAG-based architectures. The insights from the LLM vs. human nugget extraction experiment can inform the design of future evaluation frameworks. However, the actual impact will depend on community adoption and the degree to which the resource enables meaningful progress beyond existing evaluation methods.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the identified strengths and weaknesses, the paper receives a score of **7**.

The positive contributions are: (1) novel dataset for personalized search (2) complete experimental setup, (3) released code and data to reproduce the results (4) a comparison of human and LLM-based assessment of the CSA output. However, some of the weakness are: (1) the high reliance on LLM for evaluation which raise concerns about the possible biases and (2) incremental improvement (over the prior IKAT collections, and well established methods) in terms of the evaluation methodology.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### PluralLLM: Pluralistic Alignment in LLMs via Federated Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09925v1)
- **Authors**: Mahmoud Srewa, Tianyu Zhao, Salma Elmalaki
- **Abstract**: Ensuring Large Language Models (LLMs) align with diverse human preferences while preserving privacy and fairness remains a challenge. Existing methods, such as Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF), rely on centralized data collection, making them computationally expensive and privacy-invasive. We introduce PluralLLM a federated learning-based approach that enables multiple user groups to collaboratively train a transformer-based preference predictor without sharing sensitive data, which can also serve as a reward model for aligning LLMs. Our method leverages Federated Averaging (FedAvg) to aggregate preference updates efficiently, achieving 46% faster convergence, a 4% improvement in alignment scores, and nearly the same group fairness measure as in centralized training. Evaluated on a Q/A preference alignment task, PluralLLM demonstrates that federated preference learning offers a scalable and privacy-preserving alternative for aligning LLMs with diverse human values.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PluralLLM, a federated learning (FL) framework designed for pluralistic alignment of large language models (LLMs). Pluralistic alignment aims to align LLMs with diverse and potentially conflicting human preferences across different user groups. PluralLLM addresses the limitations of centralized approaches like RLHF, which are computationally expensive and pose privacy risks. By using FL, PluralLLM enables multiple user groups to collaboratively train a transformer-based preference predictor without sharing sensitive data.  The framework leverages Federated Averaging (FedAvg) for efficient aggregation of preference updates. Experiments on a Q/A preference alignment task demonstrate that PluralLLM achieves faster convergence, improved alignment scores, and maintains group fairness compared to centralized methods. The paper proposes that the trained preference predictor can serve as a lightweight reward model for aligning LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in applying federated learning principles to the problem of pluralistic alignment in LLMs. While federated learning and LLM alignment are both active research areas, their combination for the specific goal of addressing diverse preferences is a valuable contribution. The use of a transformer-based preference predictor within the FL framework is also a reasonable design choice. Comparing to group preference optimization (GPO), the novelty mainly comes from training the model in FL setting to ensure privacy.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to address the critical challenges of privacy, fairness, and scalability in LLM alignment. Current LLM alignment methods often rely on centralized data, which raises privacy concerns and can be biased towards majority viewpoints. PluralLLM provides a more scalable and privacy-preserving alternative, which is essential for deploying LLMs in real-world applications where diverse user preferences must be considered.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined framework and experimental setup.
    *   Demonstrated improvements in convergence speed, alignment scores, and fairness compared to centralized methods.
    *   Addresses a crucial gap in current LLM alignment research by focusing on privacy and pluralistic alignment.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental evaluation is limited to a single Q/A preference alignment task and dataset. This limits the generalizability of the results to other LLM tasks such as summarization and translation.
    *   The complexity and practical implementation challenges of Federated Learning are not discussed deeply. Potential issues include client availability, non-IID data distributions, and robustness to malicious clients.
    *   While the paper demonstrates comparable group fairness to the centralized approach, there needs to be a deeper discussion on how PluralLLM explicitly mitigates potential biases introduced by heterogeneous data across different groups.
    *   The use of FedAvg, while a standard approach, might not be the most optimal aggregation strategy for LLMs. Exploring more advanced FL algorithms tailored for large models could further improve performance and fairness.
    *   The fairness index used as an evaluation metric is not particularly strong.

*   **Impact:**  If the framework can be generalized and scaled effectively, it could significantly influence the development of more responsible and inclusive LLM systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution by combining federated learning with pluralistic LLM alignment. The experimental results are promising and demonstrate the potential of the proposed framework to address the challenges of privacy, fairness, and scalability. However, the limitations in experimental evaluation, the depth of discussion on practical FL challenges, and lack of discussion on mitigation against biases in different communities detracts from the paper's contribution. Given the novelty and the promising results, but acknowledging the mentioned limitations, a score of 7 is appropriate.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### VideoMerge: Towards Training-free Long Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09926v1)
- **Authors**: Siyang Zhang, Harry Yang, Ser-Nam Lim
- **Abstract**: Long video generation remains a challenging and compelling topic in computer vision. Diffusion based models, among the various approaches to video generation, have achieved state of the art quality with their iterative denoising procedures. However, the intrinsic complexity of the video domain renders the training of such diffusion models exceedingly expensive in terms of both data curation and computational resources. Moreover, these models typically operate on a fixed noise tensor that represents the video, resulting in predetermined spatial and temporal dimensions. Although several high quality open-source pretrained video diffusion models, jointly trained on images and videos of varying lengths and resolutions, are available, it is generally not recommended to specify a video length at inference that was not included in the training set. Consequently, these models are not readily adaptable to the direct generation of longer videos by merely increasing the specified video length. In addition to feasibility challenges, long-video generation also encounters quality issues. The domain of long videos is inherently more complex than that of short videos: extended durations introduce greater variability and necessitate long-range temporal consistency, thereby increasing the overall difficulty of the task. We propose VideoMerge, a training-free method that can be seamlessly adapted to merge short videos generated by pretrained text-to-video diffusion model. Our approach preserves the model's original expressiveness and consistency while allowing for extended duration and dynamic variation as specified by the user. By leveraging the strengths of pretrained models, our method addresses challenges related to smoothness, consistency, and dynamic content through orthogonal strategies that operate collaboratively to achieve superior quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VideoMerge: Towards Training-free Long Video Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoMerge, a novel training-free method designed to generate long videos using pre-trained text-to-video diffusion models. The approach addresses the challenges of high computational cost and maintaining consistency in long video generation by merging shorter video segments produced by the pre-trained model. VideoMerge incorporates three key innovations: 1) a latent fusion technique that smoothly blends adjacent denoising tiles using a sine weighting scheme; 2) a long noise initialization method that extends and augments existing noise tensors while preserving low-frequency semantic content and introducing dynamic motion; and 3) a prompt refining strategy that uses large language models to generate more detailed prompts, enhancing the preservation of human/object identities. The paper presents quantitative and qualitative results demonstrating that VideoMerge outperforms existing methods in terms of subject consistency, background consistency, motion smoothness, and aesthetic quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-free Approach:** The method leverages pre-trained models, avoiding the costly and resource-intensive process of training a new model from scratch for long video generation.
    *   **Consistency Preservation:** VideoMerge explicitly addresses the critical problem of maintaining consistency in long videos, particularly with regard to human/object identities. The latent fusion and prompt refining strategies are well-motivated and appear effective in this regard.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The approach is designed to be computationally efficient, allowing for long video generation with limited GPU resources. The use of parallel processing for denoising tiles contributes to this efficiency.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation with both quantitative and qualitative results. The use of VBench metrics and a face consistency measure provides a robust assessment of the method's performance. The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component.
    *   **Clarity of Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology and experimental setup. The diagrams and figures are helpful in understanding the technical details.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** The performance of VideoMerge is inherently limited by the capabilities of the underlying pre-trained text-to-video model. If the pre-trained model has biases or limitations, these will likely be propagated to the generated long videos.
    *   **Complexity:** While training-free, VideoMerge involves a non-trivial combination of latent fusion, noise manipulation, and prompt engineering. This complexity may make it challenging for others to implement and adapt the method.
    *   **Artifacts and Imperfections:** Although the results are impressive, the generated videos are not flawless. The paper acknowledges the presence of occasional artifacts and imperfections, indicating that there is room for further improvement.
    *   **Metric limitations:** The FVD might not be best suited for evaluating long videos. Although it is evaluated on shorter sliced videos with base model default tiles length, the overall metric is not the best option for evaluating the long videos.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The specific combination of latent fusion with a sine weighting scheme, long noise initialization using frequency decomposition, and prompt refinement using large language models represents a novel approach to training-free long video generation.
    *   The method offers a practical solution to the challenges of computational cost and consistency preservation, making it a significant contribution to the field.
    *   VideoMerge is likely to inspire further research in this area, particularly in the development of more sophisticated techniques for manipulating latent spaces and conditioning diffusion models.

*   **Critical Assessment of Justification for Claims:**

    The claims made in the paper are reasonably well supported by the experimental results. The ablation studies provide evidence for the importance of each component of VideoMerge. The quantitative results demonstrate that VideoMerge outperforms existing methods in terms of key metrics. The qualitative results provide visual evidence of the method's ability to generate consistent and aesthetically pleasing long videos.
    However, there are also some limitations to the supporting evidence. The evaluation is primarily based on VBench, so further experiments with other datasets and evaluation metrics could strengthen the claims. Also, it needs stronger justification for the ablation study experiments.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, I would assign VideoMerge a score of **7**. It represents a significant and novel contribution to the field of long video generation, offering a practical and effective solution to the challenges of computational cost and consistency preservation. The approach is well-motivated, clearly presented, and thoroughly evaluated. It has the potential to inspire further research and development in this area. At the same time, there is still room for improvement, particularly in terms of addressing artifacts and limitations of the pre-trained models.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PanoGen++: Domain-Adapted Text-Guided Panoramic Environment Generation for Vision-and-Language Navigation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09938v1)
- **Authors**: Sen Wang, Dongliang Zhou, Liang Xie, Chao Xu, Ye Yan, Erwei Yin
- **Abstract**: Vision-and-language navigation (VLN) tasks require agents to navigate three-dimensional environments guided by natural language instructions, offering substantial potential for diverse applications. However, the scarcity of training data impedes progress in this field. This paper introduces PanoGen++, a novel framework that addresses this limitation by generating varied and pertinent panoramic environments for VLN tasks. PanoGen++ incorporates pre-trained diffusion models with domain-specific fine-tuning, employing parameter-efficient techniques such as low-rank adaptation to minimize computational costs. We investigate two settings for environment generation: masked image inpainting and recursive image outpainting. The former maximizes novel environment creation by inpainting masked regions based on textual descriptions, while the latter facilitates agents' learning of spatial relationships within panoramas. Empirical evaluations on room-to-room (R2R), room-for-room (R4R), and cooperative vision-and-dialog navigation (CVDN) datasets reveal significant performance enhancements: a 2.44% increase in success rate on the R2R test leaderboard, a 0.63% improvement on the R4R validation unseen set, and a 0.75-meter enhancement in goal progress on the CVDN validation unseen set. PanoGen++ augments the diversity and relevance of training environments, resulting in improved generalization and efficacy in VLN tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PanoGen++: Domain-Adapted Text-Guided Panoramic Environment Generation for Vision-and-Language Navigation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the scarcity of training data for Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) by introducing PanoGen++, a novel framework for generating varied and relevant panoramic environments. PanoGen++ fine-tunes pre-trained diffusion models (Stable Diffusion) with domain-specific knowledge using parameter-efficient techniques like low-rank adaptation (LoRA).  It explores two environment generation settings: masked image inpainting and recursive image outpainting. Masked image inpainting maximizes new environment creation, while recursive outpainting helps agents learn spatial relationships within panoramas.  Experiments on R2R, R4R, and CVDN datasets show improved performance, demonstrating the benefits of enhanced diversity and relevance in training environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the domain-adaptation of a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model *specifically* for VLN environment generation.  While PanoGen already explored using Stable Diffusion for this purpose, PanoGen++ enhances this by integrating VLN-specific knowledge via fine-tuning with LoRA. The exploration of masked inpainting *alongside* recursive outpainting as generation strategies tailored for VLN is also a relatively novel aspect.  The application of BLIP-2 to automatically generate captions for initial view images to then use for domain adaptation is a useful technique.

* **Significance:** VLN is a challenging and practically important area, and data scarcity is a major bottleneck.  Generating synthetic environments to augment training data is a well-established approach, but tailoring these environments to better match the VLN task is valuable.  The empirical results show tangible improvements in VLN performance, indicating the practical significance of PanoGen++. The performance gains demonstrated on standard VLN benchmarks (R2R, R4R, CVDN) are meaningful and provide evidence that the generated environments are indeed beneficial for training better VLN agents.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of data scarcity in VLN and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The methods are well-described, and the implementation details are provided. The use of LoRA for efficient fine-tuning is a sensible choice.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are comprehensive, comparing PanoGen++ against relevant baselines on standard VLN datasets. Ablation studies help to understand the contribution of different components.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:**  The qualitative comparisons of generated environments are helpful in visualizing the improvements over PanoGen.
    *   **Addressing limitations:** The paper honestly addresses limitations of the approach (e.g., in REVERIE dataset performance) and acknowledges areas for future work.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement:**  While the results are positive, the gains compared to PanoGen (while statistically significant) are *relatively* modest in some cases. This suggests that the core generative process of Stable Diffusion might already provide a decent baseline, and the domain adaptation, while helpful, provides an incremental improvement.
    *   **REVERIE Dataset Performance:** The failure case analysis and lower performance in the REVERIE dataset compared to other approaches reveal that the generated environments may still lack the fine-grained semantic details needed for complex object-centric navigation. The paper adequately discusses this, but it highlights an area for future improvement.
    * **Novelty of Specific Techniques**: The component techniques used - Stable Diffusion, LoRA, BLIP-2 - are all established. The novelty largely lies in their *combination* and *adaptation* for the specific task of VLN environment generation.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper can influence future research in VLN by providing a practical and effective method for generating training data.  The use of domain-adapted diffusion models could be adopted by other researchers working on VLN and related embodied AI tasks.  The specific techniques employed, such as LoRA for efficient fine-tuning and masked image inpainting/outpainting, can be further explored and refined.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the established baseline of PanoGen and incremental nature of the gains with PanoGen++, the adaptation of known techniques rather than introducing groundbreaking methods, and the persistent limitations in a particularly challenging VLN setting (REVERIE), a score in the higher-middle range seems appropriate. It's a solid engineering contribution with clear benefits and comprehensive evaluation.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cosh-DiT: Co-Speech Gesture Video Synthesis via Hybrid Audio-Visual Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09942v1)
- **Authors**: Yasheng Sun, Zhiliang Xu, Hang Zhou, Jiazhi Guan, Quanwei Yang, Kaisiyuan Wang, Borong Liang, Yingying Li, Haocheng Feng, Jingdong Wang, Ziwei Liu, Koike Hideki
- **Abstract**: Co-speech gesture video synthesis is a challenging task that requires both probabilistic modeling of human gestures and the synthesis of realistic images that align with the rhythmic nuances of speech. To address these challenges, we propose Cosh-DiT, a Co-speech gesture video system with hybrid Diffusion Transformers that perform audio-to-motion and motion-to-video synthesis using discrete and continuous diffusion modeling, respectively. First, we introduce an audio Diffusion Transformer (Cosh-DiT-A) to synthesize expressive gesture dynamics synchronized with speech rhythms. To capture upper body, facial, and hand movement priors, we employ vector-quantized variational autoencoders (VQ-VAEs) to jointly learn their dependencies within a discrete latent space. Then, for realistic video synthesis conditioned on the generated speech-driven motion, we design a visual Diffusion Transformer (Cosh-DiT-V) that effectively integrates spatial and temporal contexts. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our framework consistently generates lifelike videos with expressive facial expressions and natural, smooth gestures that align seamlessly with speech.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Cosh-DiT: Co-Speech Gesture Video Synthesis via Hybrid Audio-Visual Diffusion Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Cosh-DiT, a system for synthesizing realistic co-speech gesture videos. It employs a two-stage approach: first, an audio Diffusion Transformer (Cosh-DiT-A) generates expressive gesture dynamics synchronized with speech, operating in a discrete latent space to model upper body, facial, and hand movements. Second, a visual Diffusion Transformer (Cosh-DiT-V) synthesizes realistic video conditioned on the generated motion, integrating spatial and temporal contexts. The system uses a hybrid motion representation combining 2D body poses and 3D hand parameters for precise motion capture. Experiments demonstrate the generation of lifelike videos with expressive facial expressions and smooth gestures that align with speech.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has some novel aspects, primarily in the architecture and representation choices. The hybrid motion representation (2D body poses + 3D hand parameters) is a reasonable approach to balance stability with detail. The two-stage Diffusion Transformer architecture, using discrete diffusion for motion and continuous diffusion for video, is also a noteworthy design choice. The Geometric-Aware Alignment Module aims to addresses a practical problem in fusing different motion representations. However, the basic components (Diffusion Transformers, VQ-VAE, mesh rendering, etc.) are not entirely novel individually, instead, they are cleverly integrated.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a challenging and relevant problem: creating realistic and expressive virtual avatars driven by speech. Generating believable co-speech gestures is essential for applications like virtual assistants, digital entertainment, and communication. If the system achieves a significant improvement in realism and synchronization compared to existing methods, it has potential for impact. The thorough evaluation, including both quantitative and qualitative metrics, helps to validate the claims. However, only addressing the upper-body limits the scope of applications.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-structured approach.
    *   Hybrid motion representation offers a good balance between stability and detail.
    *   Two-stage Diffusion Transformer is a novel design.
    *   The paper provides a thorough evaluation of performance, with both quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   Addresses a challenging and relevant problem.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty is primarily in the architecture's combination of existing components rather than groundbreaking new techniques.
    *   The system's reliance on a two-stage process can introduce compounding errors.
    *   Focusing solely on the upper body limits its broader applicability to full-body avatar generation.
    *   Ablation studies could have provided greater depth, offering insights into the impact of specific elements, rather than component removal.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Cosh-DiT system could influence future research in audio-driven avatar animation, particularly in the design of motion representations and the integration of Diffusion Transformers. If successful, the hybrid representation strategy could be adopted by others, and the two-stage DiT architecture may inspire alternative approaches to generating complex avatar behaviours. However, this would depend on future work that shows broader applications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonable advancement in the field of co-speech gesture video synthesis. The approach balances established and new techniques, which leads to potential issues. While the architecture shows innovation in integrating Diffusion Transformers and hybrid representations, individual components aren't necessarily novel. The study addresses a difficult, relevant problem that may impact research in avatar generation. Therefore, it is a good incremental improvement, but not a groundbreaking work.

### UVE: Are MLLMs Unified Evaluators for AI-Generated Videos?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09949v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanxin Liu, Rui Zhu, Shuhuai Ren, Jiacong Wang, Haoyuan Guo, Xu Sun, Lu Jiang
- **Abstract**: With the rapid growth of video generative models (VGMs), it is essential to develop reliable and comprehensive automatic metrics for AI-generated videos (AIGVs). Existing methods either use off-the-shelf models optimized for other tasks or rely on human assessment data to train specialized evaluators. These approaches are constrained to specific evaluation aspects and are difficult to scale with the increasing demands for finer-grained and more comprehensive evaluations. To address this issue, this work investigates the feasibility of using multimodal large language models (MLLMs) as a unified evaluator for AIGVs, leveraging their strong visual perception and language understanding capabilities. To evaluate the performance of automatic metrics in unified AIGV evaluation, we introduce a benchmark called UVE-Bench. UVE-Bench collects videos generated by state-of-the-art VGMs and provides pairwise human preference annotations across 15 evaluation aspects. Using UVE-Bench, we extensively evaluate 16 MLLMs. Our empirical results suggest that while advanced MLLMs (e.g., Qwen2VL-72B and InternVL2.5-78B) still lag behind human evaluators, they demonstrate promising ability in unified AIGV evaluation, significantly surpassing existing specialized evaluation methods. Additionally, we conduct an in-depth analysis of key design choices that impact the performance of MLLM-driven evaluators, offering valuable insights for future research on AIGV evaluation. The code is available at https://github.com/bytedance/UVE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "UVE: Are MLLMs Unified Evaluators for AI-Generated Videos?" investigates the feasibility of using Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) as unified evaluators for AI-Generated Videos (AIGVs).  To facilitate this investigation, the authors introduce UVE-Bench, a new benchmark dataset comprised of AIGVs generated by state-of-the-art VGMs with pairwise human preference annotations across 15 evaluation aspects. Through extensive experiments evaluating 16 MLLMs, the paper demonstrates that advanced MLLMs can perform reasonably well in unified AIGV evaluation, outperforming existing specialized methods, although a gap remains compared to human evaluators. The paper also provides an analysis of the impact of various design choices on MLLM-driven evaluator performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using MLLMs as unified evaluators is not entirely new, as the paper itself acknowledges existing works. However, the paper's primary novelty lies in the introduction of UVE-Bench, a significantly more comprehensive benchmark for AIGV evaluation compared to previous datasets. The detailed analysis of MLLM performance across a broader range of aspects and a comparison against specialized methods add another layer of novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The ability to automatically evaluate AIGVs is crucial for the advancement of video generation models. The paper highlights the limitations of existing evaluation methods (specialized, expensive human evaluations, narrow scope). If MLLMs can effectively address these limitations, it would represent a significant advancement, enabling faster development cycles and more comprehensive model evaluation. The UVE-Bench dataset itself is a valuable contribution, providing a standardized resource for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive Benchmark: UVE-Bench is a well-constructed dataset with a broad range of evaluation aspects and annotations. This is a major strength, providing a much more detailed picture of MLLM capabilities than previous studies.
    *   Extensive Evaluation: The paper evaluates a substantial number of MLLMs, offering a broad perspective on the current state-of-the-art.
    *   In-Depth Analysis: The analysis of design choices like prompting strategies and scoring methods provides useful insights for future research.
    *   Comparison to Specialized Methods: The inclusion of existing specialized methods in the evaluation allows for a direct comparison, demonstrating the potential of MLLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Real-World Complexity: Even with state-of-the-art VGMs, generated videos often lack the richness and complexity of real-world video content. This may limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   Performance Gap with Humans: The performance gap between MLLMs and human evaluators is still significant, highlighting the need for further improvements. While the models outperform specialized methods, the central aim of unified evaluation approximating human judgement needs further development.
    *   Evaluation Methodology: The translation from human judgement into numbers may not always accurately reflect the subtleties of human assessment. While using preference annotation ameliorates the issue of different annotator's rating scales, it does not eliminate the inherent subjectivity.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper's findings and the UVE-Bench dataset will likely stimulate further research in AIGV evaluation, particularly on methods for improving MLLM-driven evaluators. The analysis of design choices can guide the development of more effective prompting strategies and scoring methods.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper doesn't present a completely groundbreaking solution, it makes a valuable contribution to the field. The UVE-Bench dataset is a significant step forward, and the comprehensive evaluation provides important insights into the potential and limitations of MLLMs as unified AIGV evaluators. The paper also addresses a practical problem. For these reasons, it deserves a score above average.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploring Mutual Empowerment Between Wireless Networks and RL-based LLMs: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09956v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Qiao, Phuong-Nam Tran, Ji Su Yoon, Loc X. Nguyen, Choong Seon Hong
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL)-based large language models (LLMs), such as ChatGPT, DeepSeek, and Grok-3, have gained significant attention for their exceptional capabilities in natural language processing and multimodal data understanding. Meanwhile, the rapid expansion of information services has driven the growing need for intelligence, efficient, and adaptable wireless networks. Wireless networks require the empowerment of RL-based LLMs while these models also benefit from wireless networks to broaden their application scenarios. Specifically, RL-based LLMs can enhance wireless communication systems through intelligent resource allocation, adaptive network optimization, and real-time decision-making. Conversely, wireless networks provide a vital infrastructure for the efficient training, deployment, and distributed inference of RL-based LLMs, especially in decentralized and edge computing environments. This mutual empowerment highlights the need for a deeper exploration of the interplay between these two domains. We first review recent advancements in wireless communications, highlighting the associated challenges and potential solutions. We then discuss the progress of RL-based LLMs, focusing on key technologies for LLM training, challenges, and potential solutions. Subsequently, we explore the mutual empowerment between these two fields, highlighting key motivations, open challenges, and potential solutions. Finally, we provide insights into future directions, applications, and their societal impact to further explore this intersection, paving the way for next-generation intelligent communication systems. Overall, this survey provides a comprehensive overview of the relationship between RL-based LLMs and wireless networks, offering a vision where these domains empower each other to drive innovations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided survey paper:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a survey of the interplay between wireless networks and reinforcement learning (RL)-based large language models (LLMs). It argues that these two domains can mutually empower each other: RL-based LLMs can enhance wireless network management, resource allocation, and optimization, while wireless networks provide the necessary infrastructure for training, deploying, and inferencing RL-based LLMs, especially in edge computing environments. The survey reviews recent advancements in both fields independently, then explores their mutual empowerment, outlining motivations, challenges, and potential solutions. Finally, it discusses future directions, applications, and societal impact.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper claims to be the first comprehensive survey to explicitly examine the *mutual* empowerment between RL-based LLMs and wireless networks.  While previous work may have looked at LLMs *for* wireless networks, the reciprocal benefits and challenges receive less attention.  This perspective provides a useful organizing framework.  The discussion of wireless networks enabling LLMs is also relatively less explored in previous surveys. *However*, this novelty is somewhat tempered by the fact that many of the individual components (e.g., specific RL algorithms, wireless resource allocation techniques, LLM training methods) are individually well-studied. The novelty comes from the systematic *integration* and analysis from the holistic perspective.

*   **Significance:** The survey is significant for several reasons. First, it addresses a timely and important intersection of two rapidly evolving fields: AI and wireless communications. Second, by highlighting the mutual benefits, it encourages a more holistic and synergistic research agenda. Third, the identification of challenges and potential solutions offers a useful roadmap for researchers and practitioners. *However*, the treatment of societal impact, while acknowledging ethical concerns, is somewhat superficial and could be explored in greater depth. Moreover, specific novel solutions are not presented in the paper, instead it provides a review and aggregation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive overview of both wireless networks and RL-based LLMs.
    *   Clearly articulated motivations for the mutual empowerment.
    *   Well-structured presentation of challenges and potential solutions.
    *   Identification of key future directions.
    *   Well-researched and referenced.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The discussion of societal impacts could be more in-depth.
    *   Lacks in the proposition of novel methods, instead summarizing.
    *   Some sections feel somewhat generic, particularly when describing basic RL or wireless networking concepts.
    *   The paper would benefit from quantitative insights or comparative analyses when discussing the various challenges and solutions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The survey has the potential to influence research by:
    *   Encouraging researchers to consider a more holistic view of the LLM-wireless network interplay.
    *   Providing a framework for future research in this area.
    *   Inspiring the development of novel solutions to the identified challenges.
    *   Raising awareness of the ethical and societal implications of this technology.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The score reflects the paper's solid contribution as a well-organized and comprehensive survey that highlights an important and under-explored area. The novelty comes from the integrative perspective rather than breakthroughs in any specific area. While the paper is strong in identifying the key issues and opportunities, it could be enhanced by going deeper into certain areas, such as societal impact, or providing a more critical comparison of the presented solutions or techniques. This is what constrains its score from being higher.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10
