### Modeling Thousands of Human Annotators for Generalizable Text-to-Image Person Re-identification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09962v1)
- **Authors**: Jiayu Jiang, Changxing Ding, Wentao Tan, Junhong Wang, Jin Tao, Xiangmin Xu
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image person re-identification (ReID) aims to retrieve the images of an interested person based on textual descriptions. One main challenge for this task is the high cost in manually annotating large-scale databases, which affects the generalization ability of ReID models. Recent works handle this problem by leveraging Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to describe pedestrian images automatically. However, the captions produced by MLLMs lack diversity in description styles. To address this issue, we propose a Human Annotator Modeling (HAM) approach to enable MLLMs to mimic the description styles of thousands of human annotators. Specifically, we first extract style features from human textual descriptions and perform clustering on them. This allows us to group textual descriptions with similar styles into the same cluster. Then, we employ a prompt to represent each of these clusters and apply prompt learning to mimic the description styles of different human annotators. Furthermore, we define a style feature space and perform uniform sampling in this space to obtain more diverse clustering prototypes, which further enriches the diversity of the MLLM-generated captions. Finally, we adopt HAM to automatically annotate a massive-scale database for text-to-image ReID. Extensive experiments on this database demonstrate that it significantly improves the generalization ability of ReID models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Modeling Thousands of Human Annotators for Generalizable Text-to-Image Person Re-identification":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited diversity in captions generated by Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) when used for text-to-image person re-identification (ReID).  The authors propose a Human Annotator Modeling (HAM) approach. HAM aims to mimic the description styles of numerous human annotators to enrich the diversity of captions. The approach involves extracting style features from human-annotated descriptions, clustering these features using a Uniform Prototype Sampling (UPS) technique to ensure a more even distribution of cluster centers, and using these clusters to guide prompt learning for an MLLM.  Finally, they create a large-scale database, HAM-PEDES, using this process and demonstrate its effectiveness in improving the generalization ability of ReID models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of modeling human annotator styles to improve caption diversity is novel. Previous work has focused on using handcrafted templates or simple attribute filling. The HAM approach with UPS provides a more sophisticated and data-driven method to capture the nuance of human language, offering a more granular control over caption style than existing solutions. The combination of style feature extraction, clustering, prompt learning and uniform sampling is a comprehensive and technically sound methodology.

* **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from the fact that text-to-image ReID is hampered by a lack of diverse training data and the inherent bias of using MLLMs to generate synthetic annotations. By tackling this directly and providing a method to generate more generalizable and diverse training data, the paper offers a potential pathway to improve the performance of ReID systems in real-world scenarios.  The creation of the HAM-PEDES dataset contributes significantly to the research community by providing a benchmark for future research. The direct transfer experiments demonstrate that a ReID model trained on HAM-PEDES outperforms existing datasets in terms of cross-domain generalization. The code availability further enhances the impact of this work.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Sound Methodology:**  The HAM approach is technically well-executed and combines multiple techniques effectively.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper presents a thorough set of ablation studies and comparisons to state-of-the-art methods, demonstrating the effectiveness of each component of the proposed method.
    * **Practical Contribution:**  The creation and release of HAM-PEDES are a valuable contribution to the research community.
    * **Well-written and organized:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on CLIP and LLMs:** The approach relies heavily on the performance of the underlying CLIP and LLM models. The specific design choices for these models (e.g., Qwen) may influence the results. Furthermore, the choice of LLM may inherently restrict the stylistic diversity of captions that are achievable.
    * **Scalability Considerations:** While UPS addresses the limitations of traditional clustering methods by uniformly sampling across the style feature space to encourage diverse representations and annotations, the effectiveness of uniformly sampling diminishes as dimensionality increases. Furthermore, the increased complexity of clustering in higher dimensional space and generating more samples poses challenges for scalability to extremely large datasets.
    * **Noise in Style Extraction:** While the LLM is used to remove identity-specific words from the descriptions, the extracted style features might still contain some subtle identity information, leading to a potential bias.
    * **Lack of Discussion on Failure Cases:** The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of failure cases and limitations of the proposed approach. Providing specific examples of where HAM doesn't work well would offer further insights.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research in text-to-image ReID, particularly in the area of dataset generation and MLLM-based annotation. It also sets a solid benchmark for MLLM-based annotations for ReID datasets in terms of cross-domain performance.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some limitations related to the inherent challenges of relying on pretrained models and scaling to extremely large datasets, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses.  The novelty and significance of the HAM approach and the creation of HAM-PEDES contribute substantially to the field, offering a more comprehensive and data-driven way to handle caption generation for ReID. The thorough experimental evaluation is also a great strength. Therefore a score of 8 is warranted.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ExtremeAIGC: Benchmarking LMM Vulnerability to AI-Generated Extremist Content
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09964v1)
- **Authors**: Bhavik Chandna, Mariam Aboujenane, Usman Naseem
- **Abstract**: Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) are increasingly vulnerable to AI-generated extremist content, including photorealistic images and text, which can be used to bypass safety mechanisms and generate harmful outputs. However, existing datasets for evaluating LMM robustness offer limited exploration of extremist content, often lacking AI-generated images, diverse image generation models, and comprehensive coverage of historical events, which hinders a complete assessment of model vulnerabilities. To fill this gap, we introduce ExtremeAIGC, a benchmark dataset and evaluation framework designed to assess LMM vulnerabilities against such content. ExtremeAIGC simulates real-world events and malicious use cases by curating diverse text- and image-based examples crafted using state-of-the-art image generation techniques. Our study reveals alarming weaknesses in LMMs, demonstrating that even cutting-edge safety measures fail to prevent the generation of extremist material. We systematically quantify the success rates of various attack strategies, exposing critical gaps in current defenses and emphasizing the need for more robust mitigation strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ExtremeAIGC, a new benchmark dataset and evaluation framework for assessing the vulnerability of Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) to AI-generated extremist content.  The dataset comprises 3,141 AI-generated images based on 1,047 text prompts related to 29 major extremist events. The authors evaluate the effectiveness of various jailbreaking techniques (FigStep, HADES, Query Attack, Visual Adversarial Attack) on six state-of-the-art LMMs.  The results demonstrate that LMMs are susceptible to these attacks, highlighting weaknesses in current safety mechanisms and emphasizing the need for more robust defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies primarily in the *dataset* and *evaluation framework*. Existing datasets often lack AI-generated images, diverse image generation models, and comprehensive coverage of historical events. ExtremeAIGC addresses these limitations, providing a more realistic and challenging benchmark for assessing LMM safety. The application of a combination of jailbreaking techniques, some based on generation and others on optimization, also lends to the overall novelty.

*   **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for the safety and responsible development of LMMs. Demonstrating that state-of-the-art models can be compromised by AI-generated extremist content underscores the potential for malicious use and the urgent need for improved safety measures. The paper's systematic quantification of attack success rates provides valuable insights for researchers and developers working to mitigate these risks. The experiments help to understand how well existing safety mechanisms are really working against modern methods of bypassing safeguards.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset:** ExtremeAIGC addresses limitations in existing datasets by incorporating AI-generated images from multiple models and covering a wider range of extremist topics.
    *   **Evaluation Framework:** The comprehensive evaluation framework incorporates diverse jailbreaking techniques and automated metrics for quantifying vulnerabilities.
    *   **Empirical Results:** The results provide concrete evidence of the vulnerabilities of LMMs to AI-generated extremist content.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper describes the dataset creation process and experimental setup in detail, ensuring reproducibility.
    *   **Discussion of Limitations**: Acknowledgement of limitations like focus on extremist content, evaluation of a subset of potential adversarial attacks, and acknowledgement of the 'arms race' between jailbreaking attacks and defences contributes to the paper's credibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scope of Content:** While focusing on extremist content is valuable, it is narrower than the full range of potential LMM abuses (e.g., misinformation on broader topics, privacy violations). This limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited Jailbreaking Techniques Explored:** Although multiple methods were considered, there exist more adversarial strategies.
    *   **Model Scale:** Only 7B parameter models are examined, which may not directly translate to much larger models used in production.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust safety mechanisms for LMMs and to inform discussions about the responsible use of AI. The ExtremeAIGC dataset can serve as a valuable resource for researchers working in this area. The paper also raises awareness of the potential risks associated with LMMs and encourages further research into mitigating these risks.

* **Novelty of Dataset over Datasets with similar Names**: There has been an increase in datasets with very similar names, specifically around multimodal safey. The paper clearly demonstrates the difference of the proposed dataset to prior work.

*   **Overall:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by highlighting the importance of addressing the vulnerabilities of LMMs to AI-generated extremist content. The creation of the ExtremeAIGC dataset is a significant step forward in enabling more rigorous and realistic evaluations of LMM safety.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper is novel and significant within its specific focus area (vulnerabilities of LMMs to AI-generated extremist content). The introduction of the ExtremeAIGC dataset and evaluation framework is a valuable contribution, addressing key limitations in existing resources. The paper's strengths lie in its well-defined methodology, empirical results, and clear discussion of limitations. However, the relatively narrow scope of content and the limited generalizability of the findings prevent it from achieving a higher score. The study presents a substantial improvement of the area but a more comprehensive study involving many more attack strategies and more diverse LMMs would warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Style Evolving along Chain-of-Thought for Unknown-Domain Object Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09968v1)
- **Authors**: Zihao Zhang, Aming Wu, Yahong Han
- **Abstract**: Recently, a task of Single-Domain Generalized Object Detection (Single-DGOD) is proposed, aiming to generalize a detector to multiple unknown domains never seen before during training. Due to the unavailability of target-domain data, some methods leverage the multimodal capabilities of vision-language models, using textual prompts to estimate cross-domain information, enhancing the model's generalization capability. These methods typically use a single textual prompt, often referred to as the one-step prompt method. However, when dealing with complex styles such as the combination of rain and night, we observe that the performance of the one-step prompt method tends to be relatively weak. The reason may be that many scenes incorporate not just a single style but a combination of multiple styles. The one-step prompt method may not effectively synthesize combined information involving various styles. To address this limitation, we propose a new method, i.e., Style Evolving along Chain-of-Thought, which aims to progressively integrate and expand style information along the chain of thought, enabling the continual evolution of styles. Specifically, by progressively refining style descriptions and guiding the diverse evolution of styles, this approach enables more accurate simulation of various style characteristics and helps the model gradually learn and adapt to subtle differences between styles. Additionally, it exposes the model to a broader range of style features with different data distributions, thereby enhancing its generalization capability in unseen domains. The significant performance gains over five adverse-weather scenarios and the Real to Art benchmark demonstrate the superiorities of our method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of Single-Domain Generalized Object Detection (Single-DGOD), where the goal is to train a model on a single source domain that can generalize effectively to multiple unseen target domains.  The authors observe that existing approaches relying on single-step textual prompts to incorporate cross-domain information often struggle in complex scenarios involving combinations of styles (e.g., rainy and night). To overcome this limitation, they propose a "Style Evolving along Chain-of-Thought" method. This method progressively integrates and expands style information along a chain of thought, enabling continuous style evolution. It involves iteratively refining style descriptions, disentangling style features from content, using Class-Specific Prototypes to capture invariant semantic characteristics, and incorporating these elements to improve the model's generalization capability.  The method is evaluated on adverse weather scenarios and a Real-to-Art benchmark, demonstrating improved performance over existing techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a Chain-of-Thought approach for style evolution in DGOD is relatively novel.  Existing methods mostly rely on single-step prompts. The iterative refinement of style descriptions, the disentanglement strategy, and the introduction of class-specific prototypes all contribute to the novelty.  The combination of these elements to address a specific limitation in DGOD is a differentiating factor.

*   **Significance:** DGOD is a challenging and practically relevant problem, particularly in situations where obtaining data from diverse target domains is difficult or impossible. The reported improvements in generalization across various weather and artistic scenarios are significant, as they suggest that the proposed method can effectively handle complex style variations.  The use of textual prompts to guide style evolution and disentanglement has practical implications, as it opens up the possibility of incorporating domain knowledge to improve generalization.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies a weakness in existing methods related to complex style combinations.
    *   The proposed "Style Evolving along Chain-of-Thought" is well-motivated and addresses the identified limitation.
    *   The use of disentanglement and class-specific prototypes is a good strategy to improve style transfer and content preservation.
    *   The experimental results on multiple datasets and with different backbone networks demonstrate the effectiveness of the method.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on ChatGPT for generating supplementary descriptions might introduce some bias or inconsistencies. The paper does not explicitly discuss the potential impact of this choice, and further investigation may be beneficial.
    *   The paper does not provide an analysis of computational cost of the Chain-of-Thought compared to simpler prompt methods.
    *   The performance gains when using Swin are larger, suggesting the method is particularly well suited to that backbone. Understanding why would be useful.
    *   The explanation of some modules (e.g., the Class-Specific Prototype Clustering Module) could be further clarified for greater understanding.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in DGOD by:
    *   Motivating the use of more sophisticated prompting strategies, such as Chain-of-Thought.
    *   Encouraging the development of better style disentanglement techniques.
    *   Exploring the use of multimodal capabilities to simulate style variations.
    *   Providing a strong benchmark for evaluating DGOD methods.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper demonstrates a significant improvement over existing methods and introduces a novel approach, some weaknesses are present. Although the individual components are not revolutionary, the combination in the context of DGOD with textual prompts provides a meaningful contribution. The results validate the approach, but the paper could benefit from a deeper exploration of the reliance on ChatGPT and the computational cost. Therefore the assigned score is a reflection of these points.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Equations to Insights: Unraveling Symbolic Structures in PDEs with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09986v1)
- **Authors**: Rohan Bhatnagar, Ling Liang, Krish Patel, Haizhao Yang
- **Abstract**: Motivated by the remarkable success of artificial intelligence (AI) across diverse fields, the application of AI to solve scientific problems-often formulated as partial differential equations (PDEs)-has garnered increasing attention. While most existing research concentrates on theoretical properties (such as well-posedness, regularity, and continuity) of the solutions, alongside direct AI-driven methods for solving PDEs, the challenge of uncovering symbolic relationships within these equations remains largely unexplored. In this paper, we propose leveraging large language models (LLMs) to learn such symbolic relationships. Our results demonstrate that LLMs can effectively predict the operators involved in PDE solutions by utilizing the symbolic information in the PDEs. Furthermore, we show that discovering these symbolic relationships can substantially improve both the efficiency and accuracy of the finite expression method for finding analytical approximation of PDE solutions, delivering a fully interpretable solution pipeline. This work opens new avenues for understanding the symbolic structure of scientific problems and advancing their solution processes.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "From Equations to Insights: Unraveling Symbolic Structures in PDEs with LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel method for uncovering symbolic relationships within partial differential equations (PDEs) by leveraging large language models (LLMs).  The approach involves: (1) generating a structured dataset of symbolic expressions from various PDE types, (2) fine-tuning LLMs (specifically BART and T5) to predict the operator sets present in PDE solutions given the PDE type, boundary conditions, and right-hand side functions, and (3) integrating the LLM-predicted operator sets into the Finite Expression Method (FEX), a reinforcement learning-based symbolic regression technique, to improve its efficiency and accuracy. The key idea is that by using LLMs to narrow down the operator search space for FEX, the overall computational cost and solution accuracy can be improved. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of this approach through both sanity checks on simple PDEs and more comprehensive experiments on randomly generated Poisson equations and linear conservation laws.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of LLMs with a symbolic regression method (FEX) for solving PDEs. While LLMs have been used for various scientific tasks, including equation discovery, using them as a pre-processing step to guide the operator selection for a method like FEX is a relatively novel idea. The core contribution is not just *applying* LLMs, but strategically using them to enhance a specific, interpretable solver. Further, the creation of a pipeline to train the LLM to predict operator sets based on the PDE description is a significant contribution

**Significance:** The significance of this work is two-fold:

1.  **Improved PDE Solving:** The results demonstrate that the LLM-guided FEX can significantly reduce computational costs (number of iterations and computation time) while maintaining or even improving solution accuracy compared to the original FEX. This is crucial for tackling high-dimensional PDEs where computational resources are a major bottleneck. It's important to note that the method still relies on FEX, a somewhat specialized symbolic regression approach, so the benefits are tied to the strengths of FEX itself.
2.  **Interpretability:** The paper advocates for interpretable solutions (via FEX) rather than "black box" neural network solutions. By uncovering the symbolic structure of PDE solutions, the method provides insights into the underlying physics and allows for further analysis and understanding. This emphasis on interpretability is a valuable contribution, especially as AI becomes more prevalent in scientific domains.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of uncovering symbolic relationships in PDEs and proposes a well-motivated solution.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The steps involved in data generation, model fine-tuning, and integration with FEX are clearly described and easy to follow.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach, showing significant improvements in both efficiency and accuracy. The use of sanity checks and more comprehensive experiments on randomly generated PDEs strengthens the validity of the findings.
*   **Emphasis on Interpretability:** The paper champions interpretable solutions, which is a valuable contribution to the field.
*   **Strong Writing & Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to understand.
*   **Focus on Operators** By focusing on predicting and learning operator sets instead of direct symbolic regression, the model can learn useful features about PDEs solutions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on FEX:** The method's performance is inherently tied to the performance and limitations of FEX. FEX may not be suitable for all types of PDEs or complex solution structures. The limited performance of LLM-informed FEX in some cases points out the shortcomings of using a black-box method to tune an interpretable one.
*   **Limited PDE Types:** The experimental evaluation is limited to Poisson equations and linear conservation laws. While these are important examples, it's unclear how well the method would generalize to other PDE types with more complex solution structures. The authors should present a more diverse set of examples.
*   **LLM Training Data:** The quality of the training data for the LLM is critical.  Randomly generating equations might introduce biases or artificial correlations that limit the model's generalizability to real-world PDEs. The range of boundary conditions might be overly narrow.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of AI-driven PDE solving by:

*   Encouraging the development of more interpretable AI methods for solving scientific problems.
*   Demonstrating the benefits of combining LLMs with existing symbolic regression techniques.
*   Providing a framework for uncovering symbolic relationships in PDEs, which can lead to a better understanding of the underlying physics.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning this paper a score of **7.5**.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem. The idea of using LLMs to guide symbolic regression is innovative and yields impressive improvements in efficiency and accuracy. The paper's strong experimental results and emphasis on interpretability further contribute to its value. However, the reliance on FEX and the limited scope of the experimental evaluation (PDE types, training data) slightly limit the paper's overall impact and generalizability. Future work should focus on addressing these limitations to further enhance the method's applicability to a wider range of scientific problems. The solid methodology, novelty, and potential for impact are definitely present.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Channel-wise Noise Scheduled Diffusion for Inverse Rendering in Indoor Scenes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09993v1)
- **Authors**: JunYong Choi, Min-Cheol Sagong, SeokYeong Lee, Seung-Won Jung, Ig-Jae Kim, Junghyun Cho
- **Abstract**: We propose a diffusion-based inverse rendering framework that decomposes a single RGB image into geometry, material, and lighting. Inverse rendering is inherently ill-posed, making it difficult to predict a single accurate solution. To address this challenge, recent generative model-based methods aim to present a range of possible solutions. However, finding a single accurate solution and generating diverse solutions can be conflicting. In this paper, we propose a channel-wise noise scheduling approach that allows a single diffusion model architecture to achieve two conflicting objectives. The resulting two diffusion models, trained with different channel-wise noise schedules, can predict a single highly accurate solution and present multiple possible solutions. The experimental results demonstrate the superiority of our two models in terms of both diversity and accuracy, which translates to enhanced performance in downstream applications such as object insertion and material editing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a diffusion-based inverse rendering framework designed to decompose a single RGB image into geometry, material, and lighting components. The key contribution is a channel-wise noise scheduling approach applied within a diffusion model to balance the often-competing objectives of accuracy and diversity in the generated solutions. The framework utilizes two distinct diffusion models: a Progressive Diffusion Model (PDM) prioritizing diversity with a large timestep (T), and a Switchable Diffusion Model (SDM) emphasizing accuracy with a smaller T and a channel-wise noise scheduling technique. Experiments demonstrate the superiority of their models in terms of both diversity and accuracy, leading to improved performance in applications such as object insertion and material editing. The framework also includes an implicit lighting representation (ILR) and an RGB-guided super-resolution model (SRM) to improve lighting quality and upsample the generated modalities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the channel-wise noise scheduling approach for balancing accuracy and diversity within a diffusion-based inverse rendering framework. While diffusion models have been applied to inverse rendering before, the explicit control over modality generation through channel-wise noise scheduling appears to be a novel contribution. The dual-model approach (PDM and SDM) and the use of implicit lighting representation are also incremental novelties. The RGB-guided super-resolution is less novel.
*   **Significance:** The ability to generate both accurate and diverse inverse rendering solutions has significant implications for downstream applications. For example, enhanced material editing and realistic object insertion become more achievable. The improvements demonstrated in the experiments, compared to existing baselines, suggest a tangible advancement in the field.  The ILR component to handle per-pixel environment maps addresses a limitation of some previous approaches.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the trade-off between accuracy and diversity in inverse rendering.
    *   **Technically sound approach:** The channel-wise noise scheduling is a well-motivated and implemented technique.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes extensive quantitative and qualitative evaluations on both synthetic and real-world datasets, comparing against strong baselines. The AMT user study adds further credibility to the results.
    *   **Addresses limitations of prior work:** Explicitly addresses the limitations of latent diffusion models (LDMs) for high-dimensional data like per-pixel lighting and inter-modality dependencies.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The framework involves multiple components (two diffusion models, ILR, SRM), which could increase the implementation complexity. While the results are impressive, the complexity is non-trivial.
    *   **Reliance on synthetic data:**  While real-world results are presented, training heavily relies on synthetic datasets (OpenRooms FF). Generalization to more diverse and unconstrained real-world scenes may still be a challenge.
    *   **Incremental novelty in some components:** While the channel-wise scheduling is novel, the ILR and SRM are somewhat incremental, building on existing techniques.
    *   **Limited details about compute**: More details of what time was required to train both nets and the sampling time on different resolutions would enhance this work.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in inverse rendering, particularly in how diversity and accuracy are balanced in generative models. The channel-wise noise scheduling approach could be adopted in other multi-modal generation tasks. However, the complexity of the framework might limit its widespread adoption.

**Justification for the score:**

Considering the above, the paper demonstrates solid novelty and significance within the field of inverse rendering.  The channel-wise noise scheduling approach is a compelling way to address the accuracy-diversity trade-off, and the experimental results showcase clear improvements over existing baselines. However, the framework's complexity and reliance on synthetic data slightly limit its overall impact. The individual contributions of ILR and SRM are less significant than the overall framework. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the paper's strong contributions and potential impact balanced against its limitations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TIME: Temporal-sensitive Multi-dimensional Instruction Tuning and Benchmarking for Video-LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09994v1)
- **Authors**: Yunxiao Wang, Meng Liu, Rui Shao, Haoyu Zhang, Bin Wen, Fan Yang, Tingting Gao, Di Zhang, Liqiang Nie
- **Abstract**: Video large language models have achieved remarkable performance in tasks such as video question answering, however, their temporal understanding remains suboptimal. To address this limitation, we curate a dedicated instruction fine-tuning dataset that focuses on enhancing temporal comprehension across five key dimensions. In order to reduce reliance on costly temporal annotations, we introduce a multi-task prompt fine-tuning approach that seamlessly integrates temporal-sensitive tasks into existing instruction datasets without requiring additional annotations. Furthermore, we develop a novel benchmark for temporal-sensitive video understanding that not only fills the gaps in dimension coverage left by existing benchmarks but also rigorously filters out potential shortcuts, ensuring a more accurate evaluation. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that our approach significantly enhances the temporal understanding of video-LLMs while avoiding reliance on shortcuts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TIME: Temporal-sensitive Multi-dimensional Instruction Tuning and Benchmarking for Video-LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the suboptimal temporal understanding capabilities of video large language models (Video-LLMs). To mitigate this issue, the authors introduce:

1.  **TIME Instruction-Tuning Dataset:** A curated dataset designed to enhance temporal comprehension across five key dimensions: Dynamic, Reasoning, Duration, Location, and Order.
2.  **Multi-Task Prompt Tuning (MTP):**  A method to integrate temporal-sensitive tasks into existing instruction datasets without requiring additional annotations, thus reducing the cost of annotation.
3.  **TIMEBench Benchmark:**  A novel benchmark to evaluate temporal-sensitive video understanding, designed to avoid data shortcuts and provide a more accurate assessment of temporal reasoning.

The paper presents experimental results that demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach in improving the temporal understanding of video-LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a multi-faceted approach to improving and evaluating temporal understanding in Video-LLMs.  While individual components like instruction tuning and benchmarking are not entirely novel on their own, the combination of a carefully designed dataset covering multiple temporal dimensions, a cost-effective prompt tuning method, and a rigorous benchmark with anti-shortcut measures elevates the paper's overall novelty. The detailed breakdown of temporal dimensions and the explicit attempt to mitigate data biases in both training and evaluation is also a valuable contribution. The MTP approach is clever, allowing for efficient use of existing datasets.

*   **Significance:**  The limitations of existing Video-LLMs in temporal understanding are well-documented. Addressing this shortcoming is crucial for advancing the field towards more sophisticated video reasoning capabilities. The TIME dataset and benchmark could become valuable resources for the community, promoting further research and development in this area. The MTP approach provides a practical and resource-efficient way to improve temporal understanding, potentially influencing the development of more robust video models. The rigorous debiasing of the TIMEBench is significant, providing a more trustworthy evaluation than many current benchmarks which are known to be susceptible to superficial solutions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies and addresses a significant problem in the field.
    *   The proposed approach is well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The experimental results provide strong evidence of the effectiveness of the method.
    *   The TIMEBench benchmark is rigorously designed to avoid data shortcuts.
    *   The Multi-Task Prompt tuning method is efficient, and cost-effective.
    *   The writing is clear and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper presents a strong case for the benefits of its approach, further investigation is needed to explore the generalizability of the TIME dataset and MTP method to a broader range of Video-LLMs and tasks.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations of the TIMEBench benchmark. For instance, are there other biases that still need to be addressed?

*   **Potential Influence:**  The TIME dataset and benchmark have the potential to become standard resources in the field, influencing future research and development of Video-LLMs. The MTP approach offers a practical and effective strategy for enhancing temporal understanding, which could be widely adopted by researchers and practitioners.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The creation of a new dataset, the implementation of prompt tuning and the rigorous generation of a benchmark show deep consideration. The paper demonstrates a strong understanding of the challenges within Video-LLMs and creates valuable resources for the field.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** This paper provides a solid and multifaceted contribution to the field of Video-LLMs. It tackles a well-known limitation, contributes novel methods (TIME dataset and MTP), and backs them up with clear experimental results. It also presents a benchmarking suite, but its performance is still within reasonable bounds, with performance still significantly below perfect (meaning that the benchmark is difficult and has value), and further, it has been constructed with rigour to avoid shortcuts. While individual components are evolutionary rather than revolutionary, their careful integration and the potential impact of the released resources justify a high score. The weaknesses do not detract from the core contributions but suggest avenues for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### OR-LLM-Agent: Automating Modeling and Solving of Operations Research Optimization Problem with Reasoning Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10009v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Zhang, Pengcheng Luo
- **Abstract**: Operations Research (OR) has been widely applied in various fields such as resource allocation, production planning, and supply chain management. However, addressing real-world OR problems requires OR experts to perform mathematical modeling and programmers to develop solution algorithms. This traditional method, heavily reliant on experts, is costly and has long development cycles, severely limiting the widespread adoption of OR techniques. Few have considered using Artificial Intelligence (AI) to replace professionals to achieve fully automated solutions for OR problems. We propose OR-LLM-Agent, the first AI agent that enables end-to-end automation for solving real-world OR problems. OR-LLM-Agent leverages the Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate natural language problem descriptions into formal mathematical models and automatically generate Gurobi solver code. In OR-LLM-Agent, OR-CodeAgent is designed to automate code execution and repair within a sandbox environment, facilitating the derivation of the final solution. Due to the lack of dedicated benchmark datasets for evaluating the automated solving of OR problems, we construct a benchmark dataset comprising 83 real-world OR problems described in natural language. We conduct comparative experiments with state-of-the-art (SOTA) reasoning LLMs, including GPT-o3-mini, DeepSeek-R1, and Gemini 2.0 Flash Thinking. The OR-LLM-Agent achieved the highest pass rate of 100% and the highest solution accuracy of 85%, demonstrating the feasibility of automated OR problem-solving. Data and code have been publicly available at https://github.com/bwz96sco/or_llm_agent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OR-LLM-Agent, an AI agent designed to automate the process of solving real-world Operations Research (OR) problems.  It leverages the chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate natural language problem descriptions into formal mathematical models and automatically generate Gurobi solver code. The system includes OR-CodeAgent, a module for automated code execution and repair within a sandbox environment, enabling iterative refinement and solution derivation. To evaluate the agent, the authors created a benchmark dataset of 83 real-world OR problems described in natural language. Experimental results demonstrate that OR-LLM-Agent achieves high pass rates and solution accuracy compared to other state-of-the-art LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper presents a valuable and novel approach to automating OR problem-solving. While LLMs have been used for code generation and mathematical problem-solving, their application to the *end-to-end automation* of OR, encompassing problem understanding, model formulation, code generation, execution, and repair, is a noteworthy contribution. Creating a dedicated OR benchmark dataset is also a significant step, given the current gap in resources for this specific task.  The use of a code agent with self-repair for the OR domain adds to the paper's innovation.  However, much of the underlying technology (LLMs, CoT, code agents) is not novel in itself, so the primary innovation lies in the specific *integration* and *application* to the OR field.

* **Significance:** OR has traditionally relied on human experts for modeling and coding. OR-LLM-Agent holds the potential to democratize access to OR techniques by automating the process. The system's ability to translate natural language problem descriptions into executable code could significantly reduce the time and cost associated with OR problem-solving, making it more accessible to businesses and organizations without dedicated OR specialists.  The high solution accuracy suggests a strong capability to generate usable solutions. However, further testing in real-world scenarios and with diverse OR problems is necessary to truly gauge its practical impact.  The performance gains reported relative to baseline LLMs, while significant, need to be assessed in the context of the rapid development of LLMs and the potential for future LLMs to achieve similar performance without specialized agents.

* **Strengths:**
    * **End-to-end automation:** The paper addresses the entire OR solution process, from problem understanding to solution generation.
    * **Reasoning-driven approach:** It leverages LLMs' reasoning capabilities to ensure accuracy and interpretability.
    * **OR-CodeAgent for self-repair:**  Automated code repair enhances the system's robustness.
    * **Benchmark dataset:** The creation of a specific OR benchmark provides a valuable resource for future research.
    * **Strong empirical results:** The experiments show competitive performance against state-of-the-art LLMs.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on GPT-3:** The core LLM used, GPT-03-mini, is now dated. Results using newer or larger models would be more impactful and demonstrate greater generalizability.
    * **Limited Scope:** The dataset focuses primarily on linear programming problems and thus does not cover the full spectrum of OR problems. Future work should explore more diverse problem types and solvers.
    * **Scalability Considerations:** The number of code repair iterations is capped. While justifiable for computational reasons,  more sophisticated repair strategies could significantly improve the pass rate.
    * **Reproducibility and Generalizability:** The description of prompts could be more detailed to ensure reproducibility.  Moreover, it's unclear how well the system would adapt to different coding environments (e.g., different versions of Gurobi, different OR solvers), as the results may be highly tuned to a specific setup.
    * **Over claiming Results**: The paper titles the results with "Automating Modeling and Solving of Operations Research Optimization Problem". This claim is inaccurate because it does not solve all OR problems, only the ones in the test set, which covers primarily Linear Programming (LP) cases. It's important to state that the models have been tested exclusively on a test set and that generalisability outside the test set is uncertain and further evaluation is needed.

* **Potential Influence:**  OR-LLM-Agent could inspire further research on AI-driven automation of OR problem-solving, leading to improved techniques for model formulation, code generation, and solution repair. The benchmark dataset could become a valuable tool for evaluating future AI-based OR systems. The overall direction paves the way to a broader access to OR solutions.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**
While the paper presents a novel application of LLMs to automate OR problem-solving and demonstrates promising results, the limitations associated with the dated LLM and the restricted scope of the dataset prevent a higher score. The innovation is primarily in the *integration* of existing techniques rather than ground breaking new methods. The results are good but require more testing on bigger and more difficult problem classes.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Investigating and Improving Counter-Stereotypical Action Relation in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10037v1)
- **Authors**: Sina Malakouti, Adriana Kovashka
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image diffusion models consistently fail at generating counter-stereotypical action relationships (e.g., "mouse chasing cat"), defaulting to frequent stereotypes even when explicitly prompted otherwise. Through systematic investigation, we discover this limitation stems from distributional biases rather than inherent model constraints. Our key insight reveals that while models fail on rare compositions when their inversions are common, they can successfully generate similar intermediate compositions (e.g., "mouse chasing boy"). To test this hypothesis, we develop a Role-Bridging Decomposition framework that leverages these intermediates to gradually teach rare relationships without architectural modifications. We introduce ActionBench, a comprehensive benchmark specifically designed to evaluate action-based relationship generation across stereotypical and counter-stereotypical configurations. Our experiments validate that intermediate compositions indeed facilitate counter-stereotypical generation, with both automatic metrics and human evaluations showing significant improvements over existing approaches. This work not only identifies fundamental biases in current text-to-image systems but demonstrates a promising direction for addressing them through compositional reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of text-to-image diffusion models failing to generate counter-stereotypical action relationships (e.g., "mouse chasing cat").  The authors argue that this failure stems from distributional biases in the training data rather than inherent model limitations. They introduce ActionBench, a benchmark for evaluating action-based relationship generation, and demonstrate that T2I models perform poorly on rare compositions compared to frequent ones. The key contribution is a "Role-Bridging Decomposition" framework, which uses intermediate compositions (e.g., "mouse chasing boy") to guide the model towards generating rare relationships. They fine-tune the model using these intermediates and show improved results via automatic metrics and human evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Identifying the Source of Failure (Strength):**  The paper's core contribution lies in correctly identifying the *source* of the problem. It moves beyond simply noting the failure to generate rare compositions and pinpoints the issue to distributional biases and how they affect role-binding. This is a significant step forward.
    *   **Role-Bridging Decomposition (Strength):** The idea of using intermediate compositions is novel and intuitive. It provides a tangible way to address the distributional bias by breaking down the learning process into smaller, more manageable steps. The two types of intermediate compositions, active and passive, are well-defined and contribute to a structured learning framework.
    *   **ActionBench (Strength):** The creation of ActionBench as a dedicated benchmark is a valuable addition to the field. The focus on action-based relationships and the inclusion of stereotypical and counter-stereotypical compositions provide a targeted and comprehensive evaluation method. The metrics focusing on spatial positioning, orientation, pose, and facial expression are also well-considered.

    *   **Intermediate Selection (Weakness):** While the authors use in-context learning with LLMs to select effective intermediates, this process could be further refined and analyzed. Are there specific characteristics of intermediates that lead to better performance? More theoretical analysis of the properties of good intermediates would strengthen the methodology.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Addressing an Important Limitation (Strength):** The paper directly tackles a crucial limitation of current text-to-image models. The inability to generate counter-stereotypical scenes hinders creative content generation and perpetuates biases. The work has implications for making these models more versatile and less prone to reflecting societal stereotypes.
    *   **Generalizability (Weakness):** A major concern is the generalizability of the Role-Bridging Decomposition to more complex or abstract concepts beyond simple action relationships. The paper focuses on relatively simple scenarios. It is unclear how well the approach will scale to more intricate scenes or abstract concepts that may not have easily definable "intermediate" steps.
    *   **Impact on Broader Bias Concerns (Strength):** This research offers a pathway to addressing biases beyond just action relationships. The general principle of using carefully selected intermediate stages to overcome distributional skews could be adapted for other types of bias, such as gender or racial stereotypes.

*   **Rigorousness and Justification:**

    *   **Thorough Experiments (Strength):** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments, including comparisons with state-of-the-art methods, ablation studies, and human evaluations. This robust evaluation bolsters the credibility of the findings.
    *   **Clear Metrics (Strength):** The introduction of the "Delta" metric to quantify stereotypical bias is a good way to highlight the core issue. The per-category VQA scores also help provide a more detailed analysis of the model's performance.
    *   **Limited Analysis of LLM's Role (Weakness):** The LLM is used for selecting intermediates and creating spatially-aware prompts, but its contribution is not thoroughly dissected. It is important to understand to what extent the improvements are dependent on the specific LLM used (GPT-4o) and if there are any biases introduced by the LLM itself.
    *   **Dependence on DALL-E 3 (Weakness):** The method relies on DALL-E 3 for generating intermediate images.  This raises questions about the method's accessibility and whether biases from DALL-E 3 are being propagated into the final results. Exploring other image generation methods for creating intermediates would add robustness.

**Overall:**

This paper makes a solid contribution to the field by identifying the cause of the issue of text-to-image models struggling to generate counter-stereotypical action relationships. The Role-Bridging Decomposition is a novel and promising approach. While there are some concerns regarding generalizability, the role of the LLM, and dependence on DALL-E 3, the paper is well-written, clearly demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed method, and presents comprehensive experiments.

**Score: 7.5** The paper is a solid contribution and has real potential impact. However, I'm marking it down from an 8 because the reliance on DALL-E 3 feels like a limitation in the method, and because some of the analysis of how the LLM helps could be stronger. The generalisability to more complex cases could also be improved.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### How Do Multimodal Large Language Models Handle Complex Multimodal Reasoning? Placing Them in An Extensible Escape Game
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10042v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyue Wang, Yurui Dong, Fuwen Luo, Minyuan Ruan, Zhili Cheng, Chi Chen, Peng Li, Yang Liu
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancing of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) has spurred interest in complex multimodal reasoning tasks in the real-world and virtual environment, which require coordinating multiple abilities, including visual perception, visual reasoning, spatial awareness, and target deduction. However, existing evaluations primarily assess the final task completion, often degrading assessments to isolated abilities such as visual grounding and visual question answering. Less attention is given to comprehensively and quantitatively analyzing reasoning process in multimodal environments, which is crucial for understanding model behaviors and underlying reasoning mechanisms beyond merely task success. To address this, we introduce MM-Escape, an extensible benchmark for investigating multimodal reasoning, inspired by real-world escape games. MM-Escape emphasizes intermediate model behaviors alongside final task completion. To achieve this, we develop EscapeCraft, a customizable and open environment that enables models to engage in free-form exploration for assessing multimodal reasoning. Extensive experiments show that MLLMs, regardless of scale, can successfully complete the simplest room escape tasks, with some exhibiting human-like exploration strategies. Yet, performance dramatically drops as task difficulty increases. Moreover, we observe that performance bottlenecks vary across models, revealing distinct failure modes and limitations in their multimodal reasoning abilities, such as repetitive trajectories without adaptive exploration, getting stuck in corners due to poor visual spatial awareness, and ineffective use of acquired props, such as the key. We hope our work sheds light on new challenges in multimodal reasoning, and uncovers potential improvements in MLLMs capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MM-Escape, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate complex multimodal reasoning in large language models (MLLMs). Inspired by real-world escape rooms, MM-Escape features EscapeCraft, a customizable open environment that allows MLLMs to freely explore virtual rooms, interact with objects, and solve puzzles to "escape." The benchmark emphasizes not only task completion (escaping the room) but also the intermediate reasoning processes exhibited by the models. The authors conduct extensive experiments with various MLLMs, analyzing their performance and identifying limitations such as repetitive trajectories, spatial awareness issues, and ineffective prop usage.  The paper includes a post-game debriefing task to evaluate memory and story reconstruction abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel benchmark with a realistic and engaging task. The focus on the *reasoning process* in addition to task completion is a significant step beyond many existing multimodal benchmarks that primarily focus on final accuracy metrics. The use of a customizable environment generator, EscapeCraft, is also a positive contribution.
*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it addresses a critical gap in evaluating MLLMs' abilities to perform complex, integrated reasoning in interactive environments. The identified failure modes provide valuable insights into the current limitations of MLLMs and highlight areas for future research and improvement. The extensible nature of the benchmark makes it a valuable tool for ongoing evaluation and development.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined and motivated benchmark:** The paper clearly articulates the need for a more comprehensive evaluation of MLLM reasoning and provides a well-designed environment to address this need.
    *   **Extensive experiments and analysis:** The authors perform a thorough evaluation of several MLLMs and provide detailed analysis of their behavior, including identifying distinct failure modes.
    *   **Customizable environment:** EscapeCraft offers flexibility in designing scenarios, allowing for targeted evaluation of specific reasoning abilities.
    *   **Emphasis on process:**  The move beyond simple accuracy metrics to evaluating the reasoning *process* is a key strength.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited exploration of the post-game debriefing task:** While the post-game debriefing is a novel addition, the paper's analysis of this task is relatively brief. More in-depth analysis of the models' ability to reconstruct the story would further strengthen the paper.
    *   **Reliance on automated scene generation:** While automation is a strength, it also introduces a potential limitation. The types of scenes and objects that can be generated may be restricted by the procedural generation method, potentially limiting the diversity of the benchmark.
    *   **Lack of comparison to human reasoning strategies:** While the authors mention human-like exploration strategies, a more direct comparison of model behavior to human behavior (e.g., through user studies) could provide valuable insights.
    *   **Limited discussion of potential biases:** The benchmark, like all benchmarks, may contain biases that favor certain types of models or reasoning approaches. The paper could benefit from a more explicit discussion of potential biases and limitations.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a valuable contribution to the field of multimodal reasoning. The novel benchmark, EscapeCraft, provides a platform for in-depth evaluation of MLLMs and identifies important limitations. While there are some areas for improvement, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The focus on process evaluation, along with the customizable environment, ensures that the benchmark will continue to be relevant as MLLMs evolve.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### FourierSR: A Fourier Token-based Plugin for Efficient Image Super-Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10043v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjie Li, Heng Guo, Yuefeng Hou, Zhanyu Ma
- **Abstract**: Image super-resolution (SR) aims to recover low-resolution images to high-resolution images, where improving SR efficiency is a high-profile challenge. However, commonly used units in SR, like convolutions and window-based Transformers, have limited receptive fields, making it challenging to apply them to improve SR under extremely limited computational cost. To address this issue, inspired by modeling convolution theorem through token mix, we propose a Fourier token-based plugin called FourierSR to improve SR uniformly, which avoids the instability or inefficiency of existing token mix technologies when applied as plug-ins. Furthermore, compared to convolutions and windows-based Transformers, our FourierSR only utilizes Fourier transform and multiplication operations, greatly reducing complexity while having global receptive fields. Experimental results show that our FourierSR as a plug-and-play unit brings an average PSNR gain of 0.34dB for existing efficient SR methods on Manga109 test set at the scale of x4, while the average increase in the number of Params and FLOPs is only 0.6% and 1.5% of original sizes. We will release our codes upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FourierSR, a Fourier token-based plugin designed to enhance the efficiency of image super-resolution (SR) methods. The core idea is to leverage the convolution theorem, performing token mixing in the Fourier domain to simulate global convolutions at a low computational cost. Unlike traditional SR approaches that rely on convolutions or window-based Transformers (which can have limited receptive fields and high computational requirements, respectively), FourierSR uses Fourier transforms and multiplication operations, resulting in a global receptive field with reduced complexity. The authors demonstrate through experiments that FourierSR, when integrated as a plug-and-play unit with existing efficient SR methods, improves performance (PSNR) with minimal increases in parameters and FLOPs. The approach avoids instability issues and high costs that plague other token-mixing techniques, such as matrix multiplications or defining global filters of input sizes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of the paper lies in the specific approach to token mixing in the Fourier domain for efficient SR. While Fourier transforms have been used in SR before, and token mixing is an established concept, the authors' unique combination and careful engineering of a plug-and-play unit offers a compelling solution. The key is using *local* filters to modulate the real and imaginary components of the Fourier features. This avoids some problems encountered when trying to directly work with global filters or complex tensor multiplications that arise with other techniques. The explicit leveraging of the convolution theorem to achieve global convolutions efficiently within a plugin context constitutes a solid contribution.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical improvement of existing efficient SR methods. The plugin architecture is valuable because it’s readily adaptable and demonstrates generality. Improving the performance of efficient SR methods is crucial for deployment in resource-constrained environments (e.g., mobile devices). The experimental results, consistently showing PSNR gains with minimal overhead, underscore this point. Also, showing this also works well on existing networks without a ton of extra processing on that networks internals is a big win. It can easily be inserted.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:**  The low computational overhead is a significant strength. The paper clearly quantifies the modest increase in parameters and FLOPs.
    *   **Plug-and-Play Architecture:**  FourierSR is easy to integrate into existing SR pipelines as a plugin. The experiments across different base architectures (CNN, Transformers, and Mamba) support this.
    *   **Robustness:**  The design avoids instability issues (e.g., gradient explosion) associated with other Fourier-based and token-mixing methods in SR.
    *   **Clear Explanations:** The paper provides clear and detailed explanations of the method, including mathematical justifications and comparisons to related work.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Inference Speed:** The paper acknowledges that the FFT and IFFT operations impact inference speed, which is a valid concern. While the parameter and FLOP counts are low, latency can still be important in real-time applications. Future optimization of these operations is required. While better than some approaches it isn't as strong as one might hope.
    *   **Limited Novelty:**  While the combination of existing techniques is well-executed, it's important to acknowledge it is not revolutionary. It doesn’t introduce an entirely novel SR concept.
    *   **Limited Scope of Experiments:** While a good range of established networks are shown (CNNs, Transformers, and State Space Models) the number of them shown, and the fact that only a small number of datasets are used, hurts the ability to test generilazability across a wider range of image types.
*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient SR methods by demonstrating the effectiveness of Fourier-based token mixing. It provides a practical solution that can be adopted and further improved by other researchers.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The score reflects the paper's well-executed engineering of existing concepts into a practical and efficient SR plugin. While not groundbreaking, its contribution lies in making global receptive fields more accessible and efficient in SR, particularly in scenarios with limited computational resources. The acknowledgment of limitations (specifically concerning inference speed) also adds to the credibility of the work.

**Score: 7.5**

The paper provides a valuable contribution by carefully engineering a Fourier domain token-mixing method into a plug-and-play unit for existing efficient SR networks. It successfully addresses the practical challenges of computational cost and stability that have hindered other token-mixing techniques for SR. The score reflects a positive assessment, tempered by a certain limited novelty.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Multi-Agent Systems via Reinforcement Learning with LLM-based Planner and Graph-based Policy
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10049v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqi Jia, Junjie Li, Xiaoyang Qu, Jianzong Wang
- **Abstract**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) have shown great potential in executing complex tasks, but coordination and safety remain significant challenges. Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL) offers a promising framework for agent collaboration, but it faces difficulties in handling complex tasks and designing reward functions. The introduction of Large Language Models (LLMs) has brought stronger reasoning and cognitive abilities to MAS, but existing LLM-based systems struggle to respond quickly and accurately in dynamic environments. To address these challenges, we propose LLM-based Graph Collaboration MARL (LGC-MARL), a framework that efficiently combines LLMs and MARL. This framework decomposes complex tasks into executable subtasks and achieves efficient collaboration among multiple agents through graph-based coordination. Specifically, LGC-MARL consists of two main components: an LLM planner and a graph-based collaboration meta policy. The LLM planner transforms complex task instructions into a series of executable subtasks, evaluates the rationality of these subtasks using a critic model, and generates an action dependency graph. The graph-based collaboration meta policy facilitates communication and collaboration among agents based on the action dependency graph, and adapts to new task environments through meta-learning. Experimental results on the AI2-THOR simulation platform demonstrate the superior performance and scalability of LGC-MARL in completing various complex tasks.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, a critical evaluation, and a novelty/significance score for the provided paper.

**Summary**

The paper introduces LGC-MARL (LLM-based Graph Collaboration MARL), a framework that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL) to enhance complex task execution by multi-agent systems. LGC-MARL leverages LLMs to decompose complex tasks into executable subtasks, generate an action dependency graph, and design reward functions. A graph-based collaboration meta-policy then facilitates communication and collaboration among agents based on this graph. The LLM is equipped with a critic module to verify the rationality of subtasks and mitigate hallucination. The framework is evaluated on the AI2-THOR simulation platform, demonstrating superior performance and scalability in completing complex tasks compared to existing LLM-based and MARL methods. The core idea is to use LLMs for high-level planning and reward shaping, while MARL agents focus on executing the sub-tasks and learning collaborative strategies based on graph-based coordination.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Strengths:*

*   **Integration of LLMs and MARL:** The paper effectively combines the strengths of LLMs (planning, reasoning) with MARL (learning optimal strategies in dynamic environments).  The task decomposition and graph-based communication mechanism efficiently harnesses the capabilities of both approaches.
*   **Addresses limitations of existing LLM-based MAS:**  The paper explicitly targets the inefficiencies of dialogue-heavy LLM-based MAS, especially in resource-constrained environments and dynamic tasks requiring rapid responses. The graph-based approach offers a potentially more efficient communication mechanism.
*   **LLM Critic for Error Correction:**  The incorporation of an LLM critic is a valuable addition to mitigate the hallucination problem of LLMs and improve the reliability of the generated plans. This is a practical consideration often overlooked.
*   **LLM for Reward Function Design:**  The use of an LLM to generate reward functions is a significant contribution, as reward function design is a well-known challenge in MARL. The modular design and step-by-step reasoning approach improve interpretability and facilitate targeted reward shaping.
*   **Experimental Validation:**  The experiments on AI2-THOR provide strong evidence supporting the effectiveness of LGC-MARL. The ablation studies are well-designed and provide insights into the importance of each component of the framework.  The comparison with relevant baselines (centralized LLM, LLM dialogue, and various MARL algorithms) further strengthens the evaluation. The scalability analysis with varying agent numbers is also a plus.
*   **Tackling LLM Hallucination:** A major strength is incorporating the LLM critic. LLMs can often produce incorrect or nonsensical plans (hallucinations), and having a second LLM to evaluate the plans adds robustness.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Computational Cost:** The paper only partially addresses the computational cost. While it reduces reliance on LLM dialogues, it still uses LLMs for planning, criticizing, and reward function generation.  The normalized token cost metric attempts to address this but doesn't fully capture the potential cost relative to simpler MARL algorithms. The impact on smaller robot systems needs further elaboration.
*   **Generalizability:** The experiments are limited to the AI2-THOR environment. The framework's generalizability to other domains with different complexities and task structures needs to be investigated. The AI2-THOR environment, although complex, has specific characteristics that might influence the results.
*   **Dependency on LLM Performance:** The performance of LGC-MARL is heavily dependent on the performance of the underlying LLM (GPT-4 in this case). As LLMs continue to evolve, the performance and reliability of LGC-MARL may also change. This dependency might limit its applicability in situations where access to high-performance LLMs is restricted or where real-time performance constraints are stringent.
*   **Graph Structure Limitations:** While the graph structure provides a more efficient communication mechanism than dialogues, it might not be suitable for all types of multi-agent coordination tasks. Tasks requiring complex, non-hierarchical communication patterns may benefit more from other communication strategies.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the empirical results are compelling, the paper lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of the convergence properties or the optimality of the learned policies. This would provide a more rigorous foundation for the framework.

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper presents a significant step forward in integrating LLMs with MARL. The use of an action dependency graph for communication, along with the LLM critic and reward function generator, are novel contributions. The combination of these elements effectively addresses the limitations of existing approaches and demonstrates promising results. LGC-MARL provides a robust and efficient framework for multi-agent coordination in complex environments.  It offers a practical solution for leveraging the power of LLMs in robotics and other multi-agent applications.

**Score: 7.5**

*Rationale:*

The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing practical challenges in multi-agent systems. The innovation lies in the combination of LLM-based planning and reward shaping with MARL in a graph-structured communication framework. This approach provides a notable advantage over current LLM-heavy dialogue systems, offering improved efficiency and performance. The inclusion of the LLM critic to mitigate hallucination is a commendable feature. The well-designed experiments on AI2-THOR validate the proposed framework.

However, the framework isn't without limitations. The dependency on powerful LLMs for planning, criticizing, and reward function design poses a potential computational burden. While the experiments show promise, further investigation of its generalizability across different domains and analysis of its theoretical properties would significantly enhance its value. Therefore, while the paper presents a notable advancement in the field, the aforementioned limitations prevent it from receiving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Provably Secure Covert Messaging Using Image-based Diffusion Processes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10063v1)
- **Authors**: Luke A. Bauer, Wenxuan Bao, Vincent Bindschaedler
- **Abstract**: We consider the problem of securely and robustly embedding covert messages into an image-based diffusion model's output. The sender and receiver want to exchange the maximum amount of information possible per diffusion sampled image while remaining undetected. The adversary wants to detect that such communication is taking place by identifying those diffusion samples that contain covert messages. To maximize robustness to transformations of the diffusion sample, a strategy is for the sender and the receiver to embed the message in the initial latents. We first show that prior work that attempted this is easily broken because their embedding technique alters the latents' distribution. We then propose a straightforward method to embed covert messages in the initial latent {\em without} altering the distribution. We prove that our construction achieves indistinguishability to any probabilistic polynomial time adversary. Finally, we discuss and analyze empirically the tradeoffs between embedding capacity, message recovery rates, and robustness. We find that optimizing the inversion method for error correction is crucial for reliability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of secure and robust covert messaging using image-based diffusion processes.  It focuses on embedding secret messages within the initial latents of a diffusion model, specifically designed to resist detection by adversaries. The core idea is to embed the message in a way that preserves the original distribution of the latent space, making it statistically indistinguishable from outputs generated without any hidden messages. The authors propose a concrete construction using Stable Diffusion and EDICT, incorporating an error correction technique that leverages the dual latent spaces of EDICT. They provide a proof of indistinguishability under a specific security model and empirically evaluate the tradeoffs between embedding capacity, message recovery rates, and robustness against image transformations. They demonstrate that previous approaches that distort the latent space distribution are easily detectable.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of several aspects: (1) the provable security guarantee (latent-space indistinguishability), (2) the distribution-preserving embedding technique in the initial latents, (3) the error correction scheme adapted to the EDICT scheduler, and (4) the empirical demonstration of robustness to common image transformations. While using latent spaces for steganography with diffusion models isn't entirely new, the emphasis on provable security and the specific method for achieving it differentiates this work. The error correction method adapted to EDICT also adds value.  The critique of previous methods, demonstrating their vulnerability despite realistic output, is valuable.  However, the specific embedding mechanism (flipping signs based on a threshold) is relatively simple.

* **Significance:**  The paper makes a significant contribution by addressing the critical issue of security in generative steganography.  Previous works often focused on output quality as a proxy for security, which this paper demonstrates is insufficient. The formalization of latent-space indistinguishability provides a stronger security criterion. The empirical results supporting the theoretical claims bolster the practicality of the proposed method. Robustness to common image transformations is important for real-world applications. The clear identification and critique of vulnerabilities in prior work add to the overall impact. It provides a solid foundation for future research in secure generative steganography.  However, the reliance on a specific inversion technique (EDICT) might limit generalizability.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Provable Security:** A rigorous security model and proof are provided, enhancing the trustworthiness of the system.
    * **Practical Implementation:**  The approach is compatible with off-the-shelf diffusion models like Stable Diffusion and EDICT.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  The experiments thoroughly analyze the performance tradeoffs and demonstrate robustness to common transformations.
    * **Clear Critique of Prior Work:** Successfully demonstrates the weakness of output indistinguishability and identifies vulnerabilities in Kim et al.'s approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
