The paper demonstrates through extensive experiments that HiRAG outperforms several state-of-the-art RAG baselines across various datasets and evaluation dimensions. The ablation studies highlight the contributions of both HiIndex and HiRetrieval.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in the specific combination of hierarchical knowledge representation and a three-level retrieval strategy tailored for RAG. While hierarchical knowledge structures are not entirely new, their unsupervised adaptation (especially through GMM clustering and LLM summarization) within a RAG framework to address specific challenges (distant structural relationships and knowledge gaps) is a valuable contribution. The paper also provides a novel bridging mechanism that enhances the connectivity of local and global knowledge.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses critical limitations of existing graph-based RAG systems. HiRAG can generate more comprehensive, precise, and context-aware responses. The experimental results support the claim that HiRAG outperforms the baselines, which include some established RAG methods. The potential to improve domain-specific applications of LLMs is significant.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing graph-based RAG methods.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** HiRAG's components (HiIndex and HiRetrieval) are well-defined and logically connected.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering multiple datasets, evaluation dimensions, and ablation studies.
    *   **Strong Results:** The experimental results demonstrate that HiRAG consistently outperforms the baselines.
    *   **Open Source:**  The code availability enhances reproducibility and adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Costs:** Although the authors acknowledge the potential for high token consumption during indexing, a more detailed analysis of the computational efficiency and scalability of HiRAG would be beneficial. This includes how the cost changes with knowledge base size and the number of layers.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** HiRAG is heavily reliant on LLMs for entity summarization and relation extraction. The quality of the KG is affected by the LLM performance, which has inherent biases. While they mention this is rapidly changing they don't acknowledge the current risks of this method. The choice of LLMs (DeepSeek-V3 and GLM-4-Plus) may not be universally accessible or preferred.
    *   **Hierarchical Depth:** The method for determining the number of hierarchical levels k, while using cluster sparsity, can be better justified with theoretical analysis.

*   **Potential Influence:**  HiRAG is likely to influence future research in RAG systems and knowledge graph-enhanced LLMs. The hierarchical knowledge approach offers a promising direction for building more robust and context-aware RAG pipelines.  It can inspire other researchers to explore novel indexing strategies for RAG.

*   **Areas for Future Work:**
    *   Exploring methods to reduce the dependency on LLMs during KG construction.
    *   Developing more scalable and efficient indexing algorithms.
    *   Investigating alternative metrics for evaluating RAG performance.
    *   Addressing the LLM biases and factuality in the knowledge graphs.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

HiRAG presents a valuable contribution by addressing key limitations in graph-based RAG systems. The hierarchical knowledge representation, the three-level retrieval, and the comprehensive experiments make this paper a solid advancement in the field. The hierarchical indexing method that makes it unique enhances entity relationships, which is a significant strength. While the reliance on LLMs and computational costs are valid concerns, the overall novelty and potential impact of HiRAG justify a score of 7.5. There is more room to explore alternative indexing and summarization techniques and to tackle the LLM dependency, which are critical future steps.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Data augmentation using diffusion models to enhance inverse Ising inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10154v1)
- **Authors**: Yechan Lim, Sangwon Lee, Junghyo Jo
- **Abstract**: Identifying model parameters from observed configurations poses a fundamental challenge in data science, especially with limited data. Recently, diffusion models have emerged as a novel paradigm in generative machine learning, capable of producing new samples that closely mimic observed data. These models learn the gradient of model probabilities, bypassing the need for cumbersome calculations of partition functions across all possible configurations. We explore whether diffusion models can enhance parameter inference by augmenting small datasets. Our findings demonstrate this potential through a synthetic task involving inverse Ising inference and a real-world application of reconstructing missing values in neural activity data. This study serves as a proof-of-concept for using diffusion models for data augmentation in physics-related problems, thereby opening new avenues in data science.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of diffusion models for data augmentation to improve inverse Ising inference, a problem of identifying model parameters from limited observed configurations. The authors demonstrate, through synthetic data (Sherrington-Kirkpatrick model) and real-world neuronal activity data, that diffusion models can generate new samples that closely mimic observed data, leading to enhanced parameter inference compared to using limited original datasets alone. The study investigates the impact of diffusion model training duration on inference performance, identifies potential overfitting issues, and proposes a method to mitigate this by monitoring energy variance between augmented and test datasets.  Furthermore, they compare the diffusion model approach with other generative models (RBM, VAE). The paper concludes by highlighting the potential of diffusion models for physics-related problems and discusses future directions, including applying them to multi-state data, protein folding, and dynamic systems analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The application of diffusion models to enhance inverse Ising inference, and particularly within the context of real-world neural activity data, represents a worthwhile contribution. While diffusion models are well-established in areas like image generation, their specific application to this type of inference problem in physics offers a relatively novel angle. The development and utilization of energy variance as a stopping criterion for the diffusion model training is also a non-trivial contribution for parameter inference. However, the core methods, diffusion models and erasure machine, are not novel individually.

*   **Significance:** The paper provides a proof-of-concept that diffusion models can address the common issue of limited data in parameter inference. This has potential significance for areas where obtaining large datasets is difficult or expensive, like materials science, protein folding, and neuroscience. The comparisons with other generative models provide a useful benchmark for the relative strengths of each approach in this specific context. The application to real-world neuronal data strengthens the relevance and impact of the study. The ability to reconstruct missing neural activity is particularly compelling.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear presentation of the methodology.
    *   Well-defined experiments with both synthetic and real-world data.
    *   Rigorous analysis of the impact of diffusion model training on inference.
    *   Comparison with other generative models provides a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   Energy variance metric introduced to mitigate overfitting.
    *   Addresses a relevant problem in data science with broad applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The methodology relies on existing techniques; the paper primarily focuses on application and demonstrating its utility.
    *   The Ising model, while a useful simplification, does not perfectly represent many real-world systems. The generalizability to more complex models requires further validation.
    *   The analog bit approach, while effective, is a workaround. A true discrete diffusion model may offer improvements.
    *   The energy variance metric for stopping could benefit from more theoretical justification.
    *   The model assumes an equilibrium activity pattern, which may not be appropriate for certain applications.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could encourage researchers in physics and related fields to explore diffusion models for data augmentation in inverse problems. It provides a practical example and identifies key considerations for successful application. The energy variance metric could become a standard technique for preventing overfitting in similar scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel application of existing methods to a relevant problem. It demonstrates a clear benefit in terms of improved inference and proposes a useful technique for avoiding overfitting. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, their combination and validation in this context contribute meaningfully to the field. The work serves as a stepping stone for applying advanced generative models to enhance parameter discovery in various scientific domains. The relative simplicity of implementation and clear explanations provided in the manuscript further enhance the potential for the presented methods to be broadly adopted within the physics research community. Based on the points made above, the assigned score would be a **7**.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: physics.data-an
- **Score**: 7/10

### ImageScope: Unifying Language-Guided Image Retrieval via Large Multimodal Model Collective Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10166v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Luo, Jingbo Zhou, Tong Xu, Yuan Xia, Linli Xu, Enhong Chen
- **Abstract**: With the proliferation of images in online content, language-guided image retrieval (LGIR) has emerged as a research hotspot over the past decade, encompassing a variety of subtasks with diverse input forms. While the development of large multimodal models (LMMs) has significantly facilitated these tasks, existing approaches often address them in isolation, requiring the construction of separate systems for each task. This not only increases system complexity and maintenance costs, but also exacerbates challenges stemming from language ambiguity and complex image content, making it difficult for retrieval systems to provide accurate and reliable results. To this end, we propose ImageScope, a training-free, three-stage framework that leverages collective reasoning to unify LGIR tasks. The key insight behind the unification lies in the compositional nature of language, which transforms diverse LGIR tasks into a generalized text-to-image retrieval process, along with the reasoning of LMMs serving as a universal verification to refine the results. To be specific, in the first stage, we improve the robustness of the framework by synthesizing search intents across varying levels of semantic granularity using chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning. In the second and third stages, we then reflect on retrieval results by verifying predicate propositions locally, and performing pairwise evaluations globally. Experiments conducted on six LGIR datasets demonstrate that ImageScope outperforms competitive baselines. Comprehensive evaluations and ablation studies further confirm the effectiveness of our design.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ImageScope: Unifying Language-Guided Image Retrieval via Large Multimodal Model Collective Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ImageScope, a novel, training-free framework for unifying various Language-Guided Image Retrieval (LGIR) tasks (like text-to-image retrieval, composed image retrieval, and chat-based image retrieval). ImageScope leverages the power of large multimodal models (LMMs) and large language models (LLMs) through a three-stage process: Semantic Synthesis (using chain-of-thought reasoning to create robust search intents), Predicate Verification (verifying results with predicate logic), and Overall Evaluation (pairwise image comparisons). The key idea is to transform different LGIR tasks into a standardized text-to-image retrieval process and then refine the results using LMM-based reasoning. Experiments on six datasets demonstrate that ImageScope outperforms existing baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper presents a compelling unified framework for LGIR, which is a significant improvement over existing approaches that treat each LGIR subtask in isolation. The novelty lies in several key aspects:

*   **Unified Framework:**  The core concept of unifying diverse LGIR tasks via semantic synthesis and a text-to-image retrieval pipeline is novel and addresses a fundamental limitation in the field.
*   **Verification-Evaluation Method:** The reflection mechanism employing predicate proposition and pairwise comparison is a novel approach to refining retrieval results by accounting for local and global semantics.  While reflection is used in other LLM-related contexts, its application and specific implementation here are significant contributions.
*   **Training-Free Approach:**  The training-free nature of ImageScope is also a considerable advantage, as it simplifies deployment and allows for seamless integration with various pre-trained models.

**Significance:**  The potential impact of this paper on the field of image retrieval is substantial:

*   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on several LGIR datasets, suggesting that ImageScope can significantly improve retrieval accuracy and relevance in real-world applications.
*   **Reduced System Complexity:**  By providing a unified framework, ImageScope simplifies system design and maintenance, making it easier to develop and deploy LGIR systems.
*   **Enhanced User Experience:** By effectively handling ambiguous queries and refining retrieval results through interactive reasoning, ImageScope can enhance the user experience in image retrieval applications.
*   **Generalizability and Adaptability:** The modular design of the ImageScope framework allows for easy integration with different LMMs and LLMs, increasing its adaptability and generalizability.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined Framework:** The three-stage framework is clearly articulated and the rationale for each stage is thoroughly explained.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on a diverse set of datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of ImageScope across various LGIR tasks.
*   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each stage of the framework.
*   **Generality Study:** The generality study clearly demonstrates that the framework seamlessly integrates with different large models.
*   **Qualitative Analysis:** The inclusion of qualitative examples helps to illustrate the benefits of ImageScope in real-world scenarios.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:**  The use of LMMs and LLMs can be computationally expensive, which may limit the scalability of ImageScope in certain applications. The analysis mentions that Stage 2 (Verification) is most time consuming, and that further exploration into balancing efficiency and effectiveness would be good to see.
*   **Dependence on LMM/LLM Performance:**  The performance of ImageScope is inherently dependent on the performance of the underlying LMMs and LLMs. While the training-free aspect is a strength, it also means that ImageScope cannot compensate for the limitations of these models.
*   **The number of parameters in the models may be a bit too complex and expensive. Future studies should explore how to effectively reduce parameters in ImageScope to achieve efficiency.**

**Potential Influence:**

ImageScope has the potential to become a foundational framework for future research in LGIR. It can be extended in several directions, such as:

*   Developing more efficient LMMs and LLMs for image retrieval
*   Exploring new reasoning mechanisms for improving retrieval accuracy
*   Adapting ImageScope to other multimodal tasks, such as visual question answering and visual dialogue
*   Incorporating user feedback into the ImageScope framework to further refine retrieval results

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, I would assign it a score of **8**. The proposed unified framework for LGIR and the novel verification-evaluation method represent significant advancements in the field. The comprehensive experimental results and ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of ImageScope.  While the computational cost is a limitation, the framework's potential impact on future research and real-world applications is substantial.  With further optimization and extensions, ImageScope could become a widely adopted approach for LGIR.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### "Well, Keep Thinking": Enhancing LLM Reasoning with Adaptive Injection Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10167v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunbin Jin, Je Won Yeom, Seunghyun Bae, Taesup Kim
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit strong reasoning abilities, often attributed to few-shot or zero-shot chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting. While effective, these methods require labor-intensive prompt engineering, raising the question of whether reasoning can be induced without reliance on explicit prompts. In this work, we unlock the reasoning capabilities of LLMs without explicit prompting. Inspired by zero-shot CoT and CoT-decoding, we propose a novel decoding strategy that systematically nudges LLMs to continue reasoning, thereby preventing immature reasoning processes. Specifically, we monitor the model's generation and inject a designated phrase whenever it is likely to conclude its response prematurely, before completing the reasoning process. Our experimental evaluations on diverse reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that our proposed strategy substantially improves LLM reasoning capabilities, highlighting the potential of decoding-based interventions as an alternative to traditional prompting techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel decoding strategy called "Adaptive Injection Decoding" (AID) to improve the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). AID addresses the problem of "immature reasoning," where LLMs prematurely terminate or deviate from logical reasoning processes. The method monitors the model's generation and injects a designated phrase (e.g., "Well") when the model is likely to conclude prematurely, thus encouraging continued reasoning. The authors demonstrate improved performance on various reasoning benchmarks compared to standard decoding and zero-shot Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting, and also show how this novel decoding method can be used to address and improve cases of ineffective CoT prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of dynamically intervening in the decoding process to guide LLM reasoning is somewhat novel. While prior work has explored modifying decoding strategies, the specific focus on preventing premature termination by injecting a "nudging" phrase is a distinct contribution. The approach of identifying specific failure modes like silence, no reasoning, and incomplete reasoning adds to the novelty by directly addressing these categorized limitations of LLMs' reasoning capacity.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve LLM reasoning *without* relying on extensive prompt engineering or retraining. This addresses a practical limitation of CoT prompting, which requires careful prompt crafting and can be sensitive to prompt phrasing. A decoding-based intervention is also computationally efficient, offering a potential advantage for real-world applications. The insights into why LLMs fail to reason effectively (immature reasoning) and how to intervene are valuable for the community. Additionally, this approach could be applied alongside and improve prompt engineering (as demonstrated in the paper's results).
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation (addressing the limitations of prompt-based reasoning).
    *   Well-defined methodology (Adaptive Injection Decoding with clear algorithmic details).
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across diverse reasoning benchmarks and LLMs.
    *   Ablation studies and analysis of different injection phrases and parameters.
    *   Demonstrates potential to overcome negative effects of prompt engineering.
    *   Insightful observations on the underlying causes of reasoning failures in LLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a single, manually chosen injection phrase ("Well") raises concerns about generalizability and potential bias. While ablation studies were conducted, a more systematic exploration of injection phrase selection, perhaps learned from data, could strengthen the approach.
    *   The choice of the injection phrase is heavily reliant on English syntax and semantics. Therefore, this may be less generalizable to other languages.
    *   The experiments are primarily focused on English-language datasets, limiting the assessment of cross-lingual applicability.
    *   The maximum scale tested is around 10B parameters, and the paper notes that the benefits are minimal with smaller models, indicating that scaling will be crucial for improving performance of AID.
    *   The improvement may be marginal for tasks that are already performing well with existing methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence future research on decoding strategies for LLMs. It highlights the potential of decoding-based interventions as a complement to prompt engineering and inspires further exploration of adaptive techniques that address specific reasoning failures. In addition, the methodology of analyzing failure cases is useful for analyzing other flaws of LLMs.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper addresses an important limitation of LLMs (unreliable reasoning). The approach is relatively simple yet effective, and offers practical benefits over other strategies such as requiring no re-training or extensive prompt engineering. The extensive experiments solidify the empirical results and findings. However, the dependence on single injection phrases and limited scale and scope of experiments somewhat limit the impact of the approach. Future work could expand and improve AID further in order to increase its impact and utility.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PRISM: Preference Refinement via Implicit Scene Modeling for 3D Vision-Language Preference-Based Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10177v1)
- **Authors**: Yirong Sun, Yanjun Chen
- **Abstract**: We propose PRISM, a novel framework designed to overcome the limitations of 2D-based Preference-Based Reinforcement Learning (PBRL) by unifying 3D point cloud modeling and future-aware preference refinement. At its core, PRISM adopts a 3D Point Cloud-Language Model (3D-PC-LLM) to mitigate occlusion and viewpoint biases, ensuring more stable and spatially consistent preference signals. Additionally, PRISM leverages Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning to incorporate long-horizon considerations, thereby preventing the short-sighted feedback often seen in static preference comparisons. In contrast to conventional PBRL techniques, this integration of 3D perception and future-oriented reasoning leads to significant gains in preference agreement rates, faster policy convergence, and robust generalization across unseen robotic environments. Our empirical results, spanning tasks such as robotic manipulation and autonomous navigation, highlight PRISM's potential for real-world applications where precise spatial understanding and reliable long-term decision-making are critical. By bridging 3D geometric awareness with CoT-driven preference modeling, PRISM establishes a comprehensive foundation for scalable, human-aligned reinforcement learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PRISM, a novel framework for Preference-Based Reinforcement Learning (PBRL) that addresses the limitations of existing 2D-based methods by incorporating 3D scene understanding and future-aware reasoning. PRISM uses a 3D Point Cloud-Language Model (3D-PC-LLM) to generate viewpoint-invariant and geometrically accurate preference embeddings. It also leverages a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning mechanism to incorporate long-horizon considerations into preference feedback, preventing myopic policy updates. Experimental results on various 3D robotic tasks demonstrate that PRISM outperforms state-of-the-art PBRL baselines in terms of preference alignment, training stability, and overall policy performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles a clear and important problem in PBRL: the limitations of 2D representations in complex 3D environments and the lack of long-term reasoning in preference learning. Occlusion and viewpoint dependence are real issues in robotics, and the proposed solution tackles them head-on.
*   **Novelty in Approach:** The combination of a 3D-PC-LLM with CoT reasoning for PBRL is novel.  The incorporation of geometric understanding via point clouds is a welcome departure from standard 2D approaches. The use of CoT within the preference learning framework is a creative way to inject future-awareness without relying on explicit state prediction.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of PRISM across multiple robotic tasks. The consistent outperformance of baselines in terms of preference agreement, policy convergence speed, and stability provides strong evidence supporting the claims. The ablation studies further highlight the importance of each component of PRISM.
*   **Well-Structured and Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method, experimental setup, and results. The figures are informative, and the comparisons are fair.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges that PRISM introduces overhead due to the 3D-PC-LLM and CoT module.  While the experiments were conducted on a high-end GPU, the computational cost might be a barrier to adoption, especially in resource-constrained environments. More detailed analysis of computational efficiency is needed.
*   **Heuristic-Based Preference Generation:** The paper relies on partially labeled data and heuristic criteria for generating preference annotations. This is a common limitation in PBRL, but it still introduces potential biases and limits the scalability of the approach. A completely automated approach to preference learning would be even more valuable.
*   **Dependence on Isaac Gym/PyBullet:** The experiments are conducted in simulation using Isaac Gym/PyBullet.  While this is a reasonable starting point, the gap between simulation and real-world environments can be significant. Real-world validation would strengthen the impact of the work.
*   **Limited Exploration of CoT Design:** The paper doesn't delve deeply into the specific design choices for the CoT module. While the concept is well-explained, more details on the prompt engineering and architecture of the CoT component would be beneficial.

**Significance:**

PRISM has the potential to significantly impact the field of PBRL, particularly in robotics. The 3D scene understanding and future-aware reasoning capabilities address critical limitations of existing methods. The strong empirical results suggest that PRISM could enable more robust and efficient learning of robotic policies in complex environments. The paper provides a solid foundation for future research in this area. The application of 3D and LLM to preference learning and robotics is significant.

**Justification for the Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7.5 to this paper. The work addresses a relevant and important problem with a novel and well-executed approach. The experimental results are compelling, and the paper is well-written. However, the computational cost, reliance on heuristic preference generation, and simulation-based evaluation limit the overall impact. Further improvements in computational efficiency, automation of preference labeling, and real-world validation would significantly increase the value of this work.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robustness Tokens: Towards Adversarial Robustness of Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10191v1)
- **Authors**: Brian Pulfer, Yury Belousov, Slava Voloshynovskiy
- **Abstract**: Recently, large pre-trained foundation models have become widely adopted by machine learning practitioners for a multitude of tasks. Given that such models are publicly available, relying on their use as backbone models for downstream tasks might result in high vulnerability to adversarial attacks crafted with the same public model. In this work, we propose Robustness Tokens, a novel approach specific to the transformer architecture that fine-tunes a few additional private tokens with low computational requirements instead of tuning model parameters as done in traditional adversarial training. We show that Robustness Tokens make Vision Transformer models significantly more robust to white-box adversarial attacks while also retaining the original downstream performances.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Robustness Tokens: Towards Adversarial Robustness of Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel method called "Robustness Tokens" for improving the adversarial robustness of transformer-based foundation models.  Instead of fine-tuning the entire model, which is computationally expensive, the approach appends a small number of learnable "Robustness Tokens" to the input sequence.  These tokens are trained to minimize changes to the original feature representation for clean samples (invariance) while aligning the feature representation of adversarial samples to that of clean samples (adversary condition). This approach can be efficiently trained and add a layer of uncertainty for attackers. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their method on Vision Transformer models (specifically, DiNOv2, DEIT-III, and OpenCLIP), showing improved robustness to white-box adversarial attacks without significantly degrading downstream performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of adding learnable tokens to improve the behavior of transformers isn't entirely new.  Previous works (e.g., "register" tokens, "attention sinks", Prefix-tuning) explored similar concepts for enhancing feature extraction, improving context handling, or performing efficiently model adaptation. *However*, the application of such tokens *specifically* for adversarial robustness and the *focus on a small number of secret tokens* trained with a dual objective of invariance and adversary condition is a novel and well-motivated contribution. Prior work did not investigate this specific problem.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical applicability and efficiency of the approach. Adversarial training of large foundation models is computationally expensive. Adding a small number of tokens, trained efficiently with a relatively simple objective function, offers a much more accessible approach to improving robustness. The findings highlight the vulnerability of even self-supervised learning VFMs like DiNOv2 to adversarial attacks. Addressing this vulnerability efficiently is crucial for reliable deployment of these models in real-world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:** The core strength is the method's computational efficiency. Training only a small number of tokens reduces the resource burden compared to full fine-tuning or traditional adversarial training.
    *   **Performance:** The experimental results show a significant improvement in adversarial robustness (increased cosine similarity between clean and adversarial features) with minimal impact on downstream performance.
    *   **Generalizability:** The method is shown to be effective across different ViT models (DiNOv2, DEIT-III, and OpenCLIP) and generalizes to different adversarial attacks (AutoAttack), indicating its broad applicability.
    *   **Interpretability:** The authors probe the relationship between robustness tokens and massive activations in transformer layers, providing some insight into *how* the tokens are working. This makes the method less of a black box.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **White-box Setting:** While improved robustness against white-box attacks is important, the paper doesn't deeply explore robustness in more realistic black-box or gray-box settings. The defense relies on keeping robustness tokens secret, which is likely to be broken with adaptive attacks.
    *   **Limited Dataset:** The tokens were trained on a small subset of ImageNet (1600 images). It is unclear if a larger more diverse training set would further improve performance.
    *   **Adaptive Attacks:** The threat model is somewhat limited. A sophisticated attacker aware of the defense mechanism could potentially craft attacks specifically designed to circumvent the robustness tokens (adaptive attacks).
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper lacks a more in-depth theoretical analysis of the robustness guarantees provided by the proposed method.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in the following ways:

    *   Encouraging the development of more efficient adversarial training techniques.
    *   Promoting the use of additional tokens in transformers for various tasks beyond robustness.
    *   Spurring further investigation into the role of massive activations in adversarial vulnerability and how to mitigate them.
**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and practical solution to a significant problem in the field of deep learning security. The efficiency of the method and the compelling experimental results justify a positive evaluation. However, the limitations regarding the threat model (white-box only, no adaptive attacks), the training data size and the lack of theoretical analysis prevent a very high score. The paper's novelty and usefulness are compelling, which is why it is rated favorably.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LVAgent: Long Video Understanding by Multi-Round Dynamical Collaboration of MLLM Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10200v1)
- **Authors**: Boyu Chen, Zhengrong Yue, Siran Chen, Zikang Wang, Yang Liu, Peng Li, Yali Wang
- **Abstract**: Existing Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) encounter significant challenges in modeling the temporal context within long videos. Currently, mainstream Agent-based methods use external tools (e.g., search engine, memory banks, OCR, retrieval models) to assist a single MLLM in answering long video questions. Despite such tool-based support, a solitary MLLM still offers only a partial understanding of long videos, resulting in limited performance. In order to better address long video tasks, we introduce LVAgent, the first framework enabling multi-round dynamic collaboration of MLLM agents in long video understanding. Our methodology consists of four key steps: 1. Selection: We pre-select appropriate agents from the model library to form optimal agent teams based on different tasks. 2. Perception: We design an effective retrieval scheme for long videos, improving the coverage of critical temporal segments while maintaining computational efficiency. 3. Action: Agents answer long video-related questions and exchange reasons. 4. Reflection: We evaluate the performance of each agent in each round of discussion and optimize the agent team for dynamic collaboration. The agents iteratively refine their answers by multi-round dynamical collaboration of MLLM agents. LVAgent is the first agent system method that outperforms all closed-source models (including GPT-4o) and open-source models (including InternVL-2.5 and Qwen2-VL) in the long video understanding tasks. Our LVAgent achieves an accuracy of 80% on four mainstream long video understanding tasks. Notably, on the LongVideoBench dataset, LVAgent improves accuracy by up to 14.3% compared with SOTA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "LVAgent: Long Video Understanding by Multi-Round Dynamical Collaboration of MLLM Agents" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LVAgent, a novel framework designed to improve the performance of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) on long video understanding tasks.  It addresses the challenges of modeling temporal context in long videos by using a multi-agent system.  LVAgent consists of four key steps: (1) **Selection**: choosing an optimal team of MLLM agents, (2) **Perception**: an effective video retrieval mechanism to focus on critical temporal segments, (3) **Action**: agents answering questions and exchanging reasoning, and (4) **Reflection**: evaluating agent performance and dynamically adjusting the team composition.  The agents iteratively refine their answers through this collaborative process. Experiments demonstrate LVAgent outperforms existing closed-source and open-source models on various long video understanding datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of using a multi-agent system for long video understanding is relatively novel.  While agent-based approaches are not entirely new, the specific combination of *dynamic* agent collaboration, incorporating selection, perception, action, and reflection phases, is a valuable contribution. However, The individual components (RAG, CoT) have been used separately in existing works. Thus, LVAgent integrates these components with an original multi-agent framework.

*   **Significance:** Improving the performance of MLLMs on long video understanding is significant, as it has practical implications for various applications, including healthcare, education, and entertainment. The paper demonstrates substantial gains over existing state-of-the-art models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Performance:** The experimental results clearly show that LVAgent achieves state-of-the-art performance on several benchmark datasets. The significant performance jump compared to existing solutions are impressive.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** The four-step framework is clearly defined and logical, making it easy to understand and potentially replicate. The description of each process with the selection, perception, action, and reflection is well structured.
    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:** Long video understanding is a challenging task that current MLLMs struggle with.
    *   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework and the importance of using the correct retrieval threshold.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Complexity:** While the paper touches on maintaining computational efficiency, it lacks a more in-depth discussion. Also, the paper does not include a cost analysis of training or applying this method.
    *   **Dependence on Existing MLLMs:** LVAgent's performance is inherently limited by the capabilities of the underlying MLLMs it utilizes.
    *   **Dataset Dependence:** There is a possibility that LongVR dataset has the potential to introduce a bias towards the model's performance specifically on this data. The authors should consider discussing this potential bias and its implications.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in long video understanding, multi-agent systems, and MLLMs. The framework provides a blueprint for how to effectively leverage multiple MLLMs to solve complex tasks.

*   **Rigorous Rationale**

    The paper offers a combination of significant performance results, innovative architecture and integration of processes, making it an important contribution to the advancement of long video and multimodal understandings.

### Adaptive Inner Speech-Text Alignment for LLM-based Speech Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10211v1)
- **Authors**: Henglyu Liu, Andong Chen, Kehai Chen, Xuefeng Bai, Meizhi Zhong, Yuan Qiu, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancement of large language models (LLMs) has led to significant breakthroughs across various tasks, laying the foundation for the development of LLM-based speech translation systems. Existing methods primarily focus on aligning inputs and outputs across modalities while overlooking deeper semantic alignment within model representations. To address this limitation, we propose an Adaptive Inner Speech-Text Alignment (AI-STA) method to bridge the modality gap by explicitly aligning speech and text representations at selected layers within LLMs. To achieve this, we leverage the optimal transport (OT) theory to quantify fine-grained representation discrepancies between speech and text. Furthermore, we utilize the cross-modal retrieval technique to identify the layers that are best suited for alignment and perform joint training on these layers. Experimental results on speech translation (ST) tasks demonstrate that AI-STA significantly improves the translation performance of large speech-text models (LSMs), outperforming previous state-of-the-art approaches. Our findings highlight the importance of inner-layer speech-text alignment in LLMs and provide new insights into enhancing cross-modal learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Adaptive Inner Speech-Text Alignment (AI-STA), a method for improving speech translation (ST) performance in large language model (LLM)-based systems. AI-STA bridges the modality gap between speech and text by explicitly aligning their representations at selected layers within the LLM. It leverages Optimal Transport (OT) to quantify fine-grained representation discrepancies and uses cross-modal retrieval to identify layers best suited for alignment. Experiments on speech translation tasks demonstrate that AI-STA improves translation performance compared to existing state-of-the-art approaches. The paper emphasizes the importance of inner-layer speech-text alignment in LLMs and provides insights for enhancing cross-modal learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its explicit focus on *inner-layer* alignment between speech and text representations within LLMs for speech translation.  While prior work has addressed cross-modal alignment in speech-text tasks, and recent works have leveraged LLMs for ST, the specific combination of targeting inner layers, using Optimal Transport for fine-grained alignment measurement, and employing cross-modal retrieval for layer selection represents a relatively novel approach. The Adaptive component, dynamically selecting alignment layers, further contributes to novelty. This said, the use of OT for cross-modal representation alignment isn't entirely new in the general space, but its application to LLM inner layers is.
*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates promising empirical results, outperforming previous SOTA methods on CoVoST2. This indicates that the proposed method provides a tangible performance boost in speech translation. Furthermore, the insights into which LLM layers are most suitable for cross-modal alignment are valuable for future research. Demonstrating that AI-STA helps knowledge transfer between speech and text by improving zero-shot machine translation performance is also significant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-motivated approach.
    *   The method is clearly explained and uses established techniques (OT, cross-modal retrieval) in a novel combination.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating improved performance over baselines.
    *   Analysis of the impact of layer selection and the effect of AI-STA on knowledge transfer.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The empirical evaluation is limited to one dataset (CoVoST2) and two language pairs (En-Zh, En-Ja).  While this is a common benchmark, expanding the evaluation to more diverse datasets and languages would strengthen the generalizability of the results.
    *   The choice of hyperparameters (OT loss weight, threshold for layer selection) seems somewhat arbitrary and determined through limited practical experience.  A more rigorous sensitivity analysis would be beneficial.
    *   The justification for selecting particular LLM architectures (Vicuna-13B, Qwen2-7B) is not well-developed. What characteristics of these models make them suitable for this approach?
    *   The paper only provides a short case study. While illustrative, more in-depth qualitative analysis of translation errors and improvements would be more impactful.
    * The observed performance gains in zero-shot machine translation tasks, especially in Vicuna-13B, could be largely influenced by its strong English and Chinese language capabilities, as acknowledged in the paper. More investigation is needed to clarify if the effectiveness of AI-STA can generalize to other languages with less pre-existing alignment.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on cross-modal learning and speech translation by highlighting the importance of inner-layer alignment in LLMs. The proposed method could be extended to other cross-modal tasks, such as speech captioning and audio-visual learning. It also provides a practical approach for adapting LLMs to speech-based tasks. However, the limited empirical validation somewhat restricts the influence.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field. The idea of explicit inner-layer speech-text alignment in LLMs for speech translation is interesting and produces tangible results. The method is technically sound, builds on existing techniques in a novel way, and offers insights into layer selection. The reported SOTA performance on CoVoST2 is significant. However, the limitations of the evaluation (single dataset, arbitrary hyperparameter choices) and the potential dependence on specific LLM properties prevent a higher score. The novelty and significance are apparent, but the strength of these findings is tempered by the limitations in empirical validation. While the method provides a performance boost, it is not a "game changer" innovation but rather a valuable contribution to the progression of LLM-based speech translation.

### Efficient Federated Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models with Layer Dropout
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10217v1)
- **Authors**: Shilong Wang, Jianchun Liu, Hongli Xu, Jiaming Yan, Xianjun Gao
- **Abstract**: Fine-tuning plays a crucial role in enabling pre-trained LLMs to evolve from general language comprehension to task-specific expertise. To preserve user data privacy, federated fine-tuning is often employed and has emerged as the de facto paradigm. However, federated fine-tuning is prohibitively inefficient due to the tension between LLM complexity and the resource constraint of end devices, incurring unaffordable fine-tuning overhead. Existing literature primarily utilizes parameter-efficient fine-tuning techniques to mitigate communication costs, yet computational and memory burdens continue to pose significant challenges for developers. This work proposes DropPEFT, an innovative federated PEFT framework that employs a novel stochastic transformer layer dropout method, enabling devices to deactivate a considerable fraction of LLMs layers during training, thereby eliminating the associated computational load and memory footprint. In DropPEFT, a key challenge is the proper configuration of dropout ratios for layers, as overhead and training performance are highly sensitive to this setting. To address this challenge, we adaptively assign optimal dropout-ratio configurations to devices through an exploration-exploitation strategy, achieving efficient and effective fine-tuning. Extensive experiments show that DropPEFT can achieve a 1.3-6.3\times speedup in model convergence and a 40%-67% reduction in memory footprint compared to state-of-the-art methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DropPEFT, a new federated parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) framework for large language models (LLMs). Addressing the computational and memory challenges of federated fine-tuning on resource-constrained devices, DropPEFT uses a stochastic transformer layer dropout (STLD) method. This allows devices to deactivate a portion of the LLM layers during training, reducing computational load and memory footprint. The framework adaptively configures dropout ratios for different layers using an exploration-exploitation strategy to balance fine-tuning efficiency and model fidelity. Additionally, the paper addresses the challenge of statistical heterogeneity in federated learning through personalized transformer layer sharing (PTLS). Experimental results show DropPEFT achieves significant speedup in convergence and reduction in memory footprint compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.
    *   The **STLD method** is a key innovation. Randomly dropping layers, while inspired by stochastic depth for ResNets, is a non-trivial adaptation for transformer LLMs in the federated learning setting. The layer-wise dropout strategy offers a more fine-grained control compared to simply reducing the overall model size.
    *   The **adaptive dropout ratio configuration** using an exploration-exploitation strategy is another significant contribution.  Determining optimal dropout ratios dynamically is a complex problem, and the paper's approach provides a method for addressing it in the federated context.
    *   The incorporation of **PTLS to handle statistical heterogeneity** is a practical and relevant addition. Selectively sharing/personalizing layers based on gradient norms is a reasonable heuristic.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in the deployment of federated learning for LLMs: resource constraints on edge devices. By reducing computation and memory requirements, DropPEFT makes it more feasible to fine-tune LLMs on a wider range of devices, increasing its potential for real-world applications. The contributions are relevant to both the federated learning and NLP communities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-motivated problem statement.
    *   The proposed DropPEFT framework is well-designed and combines several innovative techniques.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, comparing against relevant baselines on multiple datasets and models.
    *   The analysis of the key design elements is insightful, quantifying the contributions of STLD, adaptive dropout configuration, and PTLS.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper provides a compelling rationale, it does not include theoretical guarantees related to the convergence or the optimality of the adaptive dropout ratio configuration.  Adding a theoretical analysis would further strengthen the paper.
    *   The experimental setup, while comprehensive, emulates distributed training on a single machine with multiple GPUs. While common in the field, a truly distributed environment with varying network conditions and device capabilities would provide a more realistic evaluation.
    *   The paper mentions that other PEFT methods are compatible with the STLD strategy for further improvement, yet no demonstration of it exists in their experiments.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in federated learning and PEFT.  The STLD method and adaptive dropout ratio configuration could be adopted and extended by other researchers. The PTLS approach also provides a valuable direction for addressing statistical heterogeneity in federated LLM fine-tuning. The open-sourced implementation will facilitate further research and adoption.

*   **Score Justification:** Given the novel techniques introduced, the thorough evaluation, and the potential impact on federated LLM fine-tuning, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field. However, the lack of theoretical analysis and the limitations in the experimental setup slightly temper the assessment.
    Score: 7.5

### Probability-Flow ODE in Infinite-Dimensional Function Spaces
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10219v1)
- **Authors**: Kunwoo Na, Junghyun Lee, Se-Young Yun, Sungbin Lim
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in infinite-dimensional diffusion models have demonstrated their effectiveness and scalability in function generation tasks where the underlying structure is inherently infinite-dimensional. To accelerate inference in such models, we derive, for the first time, an analog of the probability-flow ODE (PF-ODE) in infinite-dimensional function spaces. Leveraging this newly formulated PF-ODE, we reduce the number of function evaluations while maintaining sample quality in function generation tasks, including applications to PDEs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of accelerating inference in infinite-dimensional diffusion models, which are used for function generation tasks.  The authors derive, for the first time, an analogue of the Probability-Flow Ordinary Differential Equation (PF-ODE) within infinite-dimensional function spaces.  By leveraging this new PF-ODE formulation, the paper demonstrates the reduction in the number of function evaluations required during inference while maintaining or improving the quality of the generated samples. This is demonstrated through experiments on synthetic data and real-world PDE problems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary contribution of the paper is the derivation of the PF-ODE analogue for infinite-dimensional spaces.  This is a significant step as the original PF-ODE was formulated for finite-dimensional spaces and relies on concepts not directly applicable to infinite dimensions (e.g., probability density functions).  The authors carefully navigate the mathematical challenges, using weak formulations and functional analysis tools. However, the general strategy of extending finite-dimensional concepts to infinite dimensions is not entirely new in this area (previous works already extended SDEs). The novelty is in the specific extension of the PF-ODE.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to accelerate the inference process in infinite-dimensional diffusion models. Diffusion models, while powerful, can be computationally expensive due to the iterative sampling process. The PF-ODE has proven to be an effective acceleration technique in finite-dimensional settings, and extending it to infinite dimensions opens the door to more efficient function generation. The paper empirically shows that sampling with PF-ODE achieves comparable or superior generation quality while requiring fewer function evaluations compared to direct SDE sampling. This directly addresses a key limitation of diffusion models, making them more practical for real-world applications like solving PDEs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Mathematical Rigor:** The paper presents a mathematically rigorous derivation of the infinite-dimensional PF-ODE, which is crucial for ensuring the theoretical validity and applicability of the result.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on both synthetic and real-world PDE problems provide strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed PF-ODE in accelerating inference while preserving sample quality. The inclusion of comparisons with SDE sampling provides a clear benchmark.
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the motivation for the work, addressing the limitations of finite-dimensional diffusion models and the need for faster inference in infinite-dimensional settings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** While the paper tackles the PF-ODE, other acceleration techniques for diffusion models could also be explored.
    *   **Experimental Limitations:** While the included examples are relevant, further investigation into more complex function generation tasks would strengthen the results. The PDE examples, while real-world, could benefit from more extensive benchmarking on a wider range of problems with varying levels of complexity and dimensions.
    *   **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** The 1D function generation experiments rely on a pre-trained model. This makes it difficult to assess the direct impact of the PF-ODE versus the quality of the pre-trained score network.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the development and adoption of infinite-dimensional diffusion models in various applications, particularly those where computational efficiency is a concern. By providing a mathematically sound and empirically validated method for accelerating inference, the paper contributes to the broader field of generative modeling and its applications in scientific computing and other domains. Follow-up research will likely explore extensions to other acceleration techniques and applications to more complex problem settings.

**Overall:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field by rigorously deriving and empirically validating the PF-ODE in infinite-dimensional spaces. While the scope of the experiments could be broadened, the findings clearly demonstrate the potential for significant acceleration in inference.  The mathematical rigor and relevance to a growing area of research contribute to its importance.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**  The paper delivers a novel and well-supported extension of PF-ODEs to the infinite-dimensional setting. Its significance lies in addressing the computational bottleneck inherent in infinite-dimensional diffusion models. The mathematical rigour and validation are strengths.  However, the limited scope of experimental verification (relying on pre-trained models in one area, and relatively simple PDEs) and lack of exploration into more advanced acceleration techniques prevent it from achieving a higher score. While impactful, it has room for further exploration to maximise its impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unveiling the Invisible: Reasoning Complex Occlusions Amodally with AURA
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10225v1)
- **Authors**: Zhixuan Li, Hyunse Yoon, Sanghoon Lee, Weisi Lin
- **Abstract**: Amodal segmentation aims to infer the complete shape of occluded objects, even when the occluded region's appearance is unavailable. However, current amodal segmentation methods lack the capability to interact with users through text input and struggle to understand or reason about implicit and complex purposes. While methods like LISA integrate multi-modal large language models (LLMs) with segmentation for reasoning tasks, they are limited to predicting only visible object regions and face challenges in handling complex occlusion scenarios. To address these limitations, we propose a novel task named amodal reasoning segmentation, aiming to predict the complete amodal shape of occluded objects while providing answers with elaborations based on user text input. We develop a generalizable dataset generation pipeline and introduce a new dataset focusing on daily life scenarios, encompassing diverse real-world occlusions. Furthermore, we present AURA (Amodal Understanding and Reasoning Assistant), a novel model with advanced global and spatial-level designs specifically tailored to handle complex occlusions. Extensive experiments validate AURA's effectiveness on the proposed dataset. The code, model, and dataset will be publicly released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Unveiling the Invisible: Reasoning Complex Occlusions Amodally with AURA":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new task, Amodal Reasoning Segmentation (ARS), which combines amodal segmentation with reasoning based on user input. The goal is to predict the complete shape of occluded objects while simultaneously answering user questions about the scene. To tackle this, the authors present AURA (Amodal Understanding and Reasoning Assistant), a novel multi-modal model. AURA uses an occlusion condition encoder and a spatial occlusion encoder to handle complex occlusions. The authors also introduce AmodalReasonSeg, a new dataset focusing on daily-life occlusion scenarios, and provide extensive experiments to demonstrate AURA's effectiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel task, Amodal Reasoning Segmentation (ARS), which is a significant step forward. The combination of amodal segmentation with language-based reasoning is a valuable contribution. The introduction of the AmodalReasonSeg dataset, designed specifically for this new task, is also noteworthy. The design of AURA, with the Occlusion Condition Encoder and Spatial Occlusion Encoder, is also a new contribution geared specifically to this multimodal task. Prior amodal segmentation methods primarily focused on the visual domain without considering user interaction or reasoning. Moreover, AURA's ability to handle multi-object segmentation in a single conversation enhances its practical application.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in addressing a critical gap in existing research. While previous methods have achieved success in visible segmentation and some success in amodal segmentation, they struggle with complex occlusion scenarios and lack the ability to reason and interact with users through language. The ability to understand the full shape of objects, including occluded regions, based on user input has broad implications for applications like autonomous driving, robotics, and image editing. The created dataset addresses the scarcity of appropriate training data for this task, enabling further research in the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated task.
    *   Novel model architecture (AURA) specifically designed for ARS.
    *   Introduction of a new, relevant dataset (AmodalReasonSeg).
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation demonstrates the model's effectiveness.
    *   Code and dataset are promised to be publicly released, promoting reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The implementation details, especially related to the interaction between different modules of the model, could be more detailed.
    *   While the quantitative results show improvements over existing methods, a deeper analysis of the types of occlusions and scenes where AURA excels (and struggles) would be beneficial.
    *   The dependency on large pre-trained language and vision models (LLaVA, SAM) limits the accessibility of this approach. While the use of LoRA alleviates this somewhat, resource requirements still remain high.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the field of amodal segmentation by shifting the focus towards reasoning and user interaction. The dataset will likely serve as a benchmark for future research in this area. The proposed architecture could also inspire new designs for multi-modal segmentation models. The extension towards interactive visual applications is a clear path forward.

*   **Score Justification:** I am assigning a score of 8. The paper is well-written, and clearly introduces a novel task with a practical application. The creation of a specific dataset is crucial. While the paper presents a significant step forward and addresses real limitations in amodal segmentation, the implementation details regarding the fusion of visual and language information could be more refined. Furthermore, the strong dependence on large pre-trained models slightly limits the practical applicability of this approach. Future work could focus on reducing the computational requirements and exploring alternative architectures for feature fusion.
