    * **Dependency on Prompt Engineering:**  The performance of the method is likely highly dependent on the quality of the template-based prompts used for generating questions and extracting scene information. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the prompt design process and its impact on the results.
    * **Limited Scope of Graph-Based Hints:** The paper focuses on attributions and edges for generating hints, but other potentially useful information from the scene graph (e.g., contextual relationships, scene context) could be explored.
    * **Potential for Bias Amplification:** There is a risk of reinforcing biases present in the initial labeled data during the pseudo-labeling and refinement process. A discussion of potential mitigation strategies would be valuable.
    * **Incremental Unlabeled Data:** Iteratively increasing the unlabeled data as the model trains can be computationally expensive. It would be nice if the paper discussed methods to lower the computation cost.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in semi-supervised learning for autonomous driving. The proposed SCR method can be adapted and extended to other tasks and datasets. The focus on leveraging unlabeled data is particularly relevant given the increasing availability of driving data.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of autonomous driving by proposing an effective semi-supervised learning approach for improving Language Driving Models. The use of graph-based hints for pseudo-label refinement is a novel and promising technique. While there are limitations related to prompt engineering dependency and potential bias amplification, the strong experimental results and clear methodology warrant a positive assessment. The approach offers a practical solution to address the data scarcity problem in autonomous driving and has the potential to inspire further research in this area.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Long Context Tuning for Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10589v1)
- **Authors**: Yuwei Guo, Ceyuan Yang, Ziyan Yang, Zhibei Ma, Zhijie Lin, Zhenheng Yang, Dahua Lin, Lu Jiang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in video generation can produce realistic, minute-long single-shot videos with scalable diffusion transformers. However, real-world narrative videos require multi-shot scenes with visual and dynamic consistency across shots. In this work, we introduce Long Context Tuning (LCT), a training paradigm that expands the context window of pre-trained single-shot video diffusion models to learn scene-level consistency directly from data. Our method expands full attention mechanisms from individual shots to encompass all shots within a scene, incorporating interleaved 3D position embedding and an asynchronous noise strategy, enabling both joint and auto-regressive shot generation without additional parameters. Models with bidirectional attention after LCT can further be fine-tuned with context-causal attention, facilitating auto-regressive generation with efficient KV-cache. Experiments demonstrate single-shot models after LCT can produce coherent multi-shot scenes and exhibit emerging capabilities, including compositional generation and interactive shot extension, paving the way for more practical visual content creation. See https://guoyww.github.io/projects/long-context-video/ for more details.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Long Context Tuning for Video Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Long Context Tuning (LCT), a training paradigm designed to extend the context window of pre-trained single-shot video diffusion models for scene-level video generation. LCT aims to improve visual and temporal consistency across multiple shots in a scene. It achieves this by adapting the full attention mechanism to encompass all shots within a scene, using interleaved 3D position embeddings and an asynchronous noise strategy. This allows for both joint and auto-regressive shot generation without adding new parameters to the existing model.  Furthermore, the paper proposes context-causal attention fine-tuning, which reduces computational overhead during auto-regressive generation by leveraging KV-caching. Experimental results demonstrate that single-shot models, after LCT, are capable of producing coherent multi-shot scenes and exhibit emergent capabilities like compositional generation and interactive shot extension.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach to scene-level video generation by directly tuning single-shot models to handle longer contexts. While previous works have addressed multi-shot video generation, LCT's parameter-efficient approach and direct adaptation of single-shot models are unique. The interleaved 3D position embeddings and asynchronous noise strategy are also interesting contributions to achieving cross-shot consistency.  However, the core idea of extending the context window using attention mechanisms is not entirely new, given advancements in sequence modeling in other domains.

* **Significance:** The potential significance of this work lies in bridging the gap between single-shot video generation and the more complex demands of narrative video production. The emergent capabilities, such as compositional generation and interactive shot extension, offer promising avenues for more controllable and practical video content creation workflows. By building upon existing single-shot models without adding parameters or retraining from scratch, the approach offers a computationally efficient route toward achieving higher-level video generation capabilities. However, the reliance on existing single-shot diffusion models inherently limits the scope of the work, which cannot address fundamental limitations of these models.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Parameter Efficiency:** LCT achieves scene-level generation without introducing new parameters, making it a practical approach for fine-tuning existing models.
    * **Emergent Capabilities:** The model exhibits emergent capabilities, such as compositional generation and shot extension, suggesting that LCT can unlock new possibilities beyond its primary design.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed methods and experimental setup.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on Single-Shot Models:** LCT relies on the performance and limitations of the pre-trained single-shot video models.
    * **Reliance on Automatic Prompting:**  The use of Gemini-1.5 for generating prompts raises questions about the quality and reliability of the training data and may introduce biases.
    * **Limited Quantitative Evaluation:** While VBench is used, the absence of specific scene-level coherence metrics weakens the quantitative analysis. The user study partially addresses this, but more robust and diverse metrics would strengthen the claims.
    * **Limited Scope of Emergent Capabilities:** While the paper highlights emergent abilities, the degree and robustness of these are not thoroughly explored.

* **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence future research in scene-level video generation, particularly in areas such as:
    * Development of more efficient and parameter-light adaptation techniques.
    * Exploration of novel attention mechanisms for long-range dependencies in video.
    * Investigating interactive and controllable video generation workflows.
    * Further exploration of the emergent capabilities of adapted single-shot models.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a meaningful contribution by presenting a parameter-efficient method for adapting existing single-shot models to generate coherent multi-shot videos. The experimental results and emergent capabilities are encouraging. However, the reliance on existing models, reliance on automatic prompting, and limited quantitative evaluation, prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### CameraCtrl II: Dynamic Scene Exploration via Camera-controlled Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10592v1)
- **Authors**: Hao He, Ceyuan Yang, Shanchuan Lin, Yinghao Xu, Meng Wei, Liangke Gui, Qi Zhao, Gordon Wetzstein, Lu Jiang, Hongsheng Li
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces CameraCtrl II, a framework that enables large-scale dynamic scene exploration through a camera-controlled video diffusion model. Previous camera-conditioned video generative models suffer from diminished video dynamics and limited range of viewpoints when generating videos with large camera movement. We take an approach that progressively expands the generation of dynamic scenes -- first enhancing dynamic content within individual video clip, then extending this capability to create seamless explorations across broad viewpoint ranges. Specifically, we construct a dataset featuring a large degree of dynamics with camera parameter annotations for training while designing a lightweight camera injection module and training scheme to preserve dynamics of the pretrained models. Building on these improved single-clip techniques, we enable extended scene exploration by allowing users to iteratively specify camera trajectories for generating coherent video sequences. Experiments across diverse scenarios demonstrate that CameraCtrl Ii enables camera-controlled dynamic scene synthesis with substantially wider spatial exploration than previous approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CameraCtrl II: Dynamic Scene Exploration via Camera-controlled Video Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CameraCtrl II, an improved framework for large-scale dynamic scene exploration using camera-controlled video diffusion models.  It addresses limitations of previous camera-conditioned video generation methods, specifically diminished video dynamics and restricted viewpoint range. CameraCtrl II uses a dataset with high motion extracted from real dynamic videos with structure from motion, incorporates lightweight camera parameter injection, and introduces a video extension scheme for iterative video generation based on user-defined camera trajectories. The method preserves scene consistency while following user-specified camera movements and allows users to explore dynamic scenes from various perspectives.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits reasonable novelty in several aspects:

    *   **Dataset Curation:** The creation of a dynamic video dataset (REALCAM) with camera parameter annotations derived from real-world videos is a significant practical contribution. This addresses a key weakness of prior work which relied on static scene datasets and thereby limited dynamic video generation. The balancing of camera trajectories is also a novel contribution.

    *   **Lightweight Camera Injection:** The proposed method for camera parameter injection at the initial layer of the diffusion model is relatively simple but reportedly effective in preserving dynamic content.

    *   **Video Extension Scheme:** The iterative video generation scheme, which allows for progressive scene exploration, is a valuable addition, extending the capabilities beyond single short video clips. This allows the model to build context and generate consistent, longer explorations.

    *   **Joint Training Strategy:** Combining camera-labeled and unlabeled data and the resulting classifier-free guidance for camera control is clever.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to be significant. By addressing the limitations of earlier camera-conditioned video generation methods, CameraCtrl II opens avenues for more interactive and immersive scene exploration experiences. Potential areas of impact:

    *   **Virtual Tourism/Training:**  The framework could be used to generate realistic and explorable environments for tourism or training applications.
    *   **Content Creation:** It could assist artists and designers in creating dynamic scenes from specific camera perspectives.
    *   **Simulation:** Might enable more realistic and controllable environments for simulating various scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured description of the method.
    *   The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the importance of the different components of the pipeline.
    *   The quantitative and qualitative results suggest improvements over previous approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper lacks a more in-depth analysis of the types of scenes where the method excels or struggles. While failure cases are mentioned, a systematic characterization would be beneficial.
    *   Although runtime is briefly mentioned, a more detailed analysis of the computational requirements would be helpful for practical deployment.
    *   The comparison is made against other methods and there is limited evaluation around what aspects of the data are leading to the improved results.

*   **Impact:** The paper's impact hinges on whether the generated videos are genuinely high-quality and compelling for users, and whether the computational cost of the method is practical.  The dataset itself could become a valuable resource for other researchers in the field.

* **Further work:**
  * It is necessary to analyze what aspects of the data is leading to improved results. What are the specific characteristics of this dataset (as opposed to others) and how is it making the model improve?
  * Additional studies around geometric consistency can provide additional value.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. The paper presents a set of valuable contributions to the field of camera-controlled video generation. The introduction of the REALCAM dataset with trajectory balancing addresses a critical limitation of previous approaches, and the lightweight camera injection method is an efficient way to control camera movement without sacrificing dynamic scene generation. The video extension scheme is a significant step towards enabling more interactive and immersive scene exploration. The weaknesses lie in the limited analysis of failure cases and the potential for improvement around geometric consistency. The significance of the paper is moderate to high, with the potential for impactful applications in various fields.

### TruthPrInt: Mitigating LVLM Object Hallucination Via Latent Truthful-Guided Pre-Intervention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10602v1)
- **Authors**: Jinhao Duan, Fei Kong, Hao Cheng, James Diffenderfer, Bhavya Kailkhura, Lichao Sun, Xiaofeng Zhu, Xiaoshuang Shi, Kaidi Xu
- **Abstract**: Object Hallucination (OH) has been acknowledged as one of the major trustworthy challenges in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) indicate that internal states, such as hidden states, encode the "overall truthfulness" of generated responses. However, it remains under-explored how internal states in LVLMs function and whether they could serve as "per-token" hallucination indicators, which is essential for mitigating OH. In this paper, we first conduct an in-depth exploration of LVLM internal states in relation to OH issues and discover that (1) LVLM internal states are high-specificity per-token indicators of hallucination behaviors. Moreover, (2) different LVLMs encode universal patterns of hallucinations in common latent subspaces, indicating that there exist "generic truthful directions" shared by various LVLMs. Based on these discoveries, we propose Truthful-Guided Pre-Intervention (TruthPrInt) that first learns the truthful direction of LVLM decoding and then applies truthful-guided inference-time intervention during LVLM decoding. We further propose ComnHallu to enhance both cross-LVLM and cross-data hallucination detection transferability by constructing and aligning hallucination latent subspaces. We evaluate TruthPrInt in extensive experimental settings, including in-domain and out-of-domain scenarios, over popular LVLMs and OH benchmarks. Experimental results indicate that TruthPrInt significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods. Codes will be available at https://github.com/jinhaoduan/TruthPrInt.
- **Summary**: The paper "TruthPrInt: Mitigating LVLM Object Hallucination Via Latent Truthful-Guided Pre-Intervention" proposes a method to mitigate object hallucination (OH) in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) by leveraging the internal states of the models. The core idea is to identify "truthful directions" within the latent space of LVLMs and then intervene during the decoding process to guide the model towards more truthful generations. The paper makes two main claims: (1) LVLM internal states are high-specificity indicators of hallucination behaviors, encoding universal patterns in common latent subspaces, and (2) a truthful-guided pre-intervention approach (TruthPrInt) can effectively mitigate OH by locating hallucinated tokens and performing truthful decoding. To enhance transferability, the paper also introduces ComnHallu, a subspace alignment method. The method is evaluated on several LVLMs and OH benchmarks, demonstrating improved performance compared to existing baselines.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its approach to using internal states for *per-token* hallucination detection and the subsequent intervention based on these internal state-derived truthful directions. While the use of internal states in LLMs has been explored before, its application to specifically address OH in LVLMs with per-token granularity is a relatively novel contribution. The idea of identifying and exploiting common latent subspaces for hallucination detection (ComnHallu) also presents a potentially valuable avenue for improving transferability across different LVLMs.

**Significance:** If the claims hold strong, the paper could be significant for the following reasons:

*   **Addresses a critical problem:** Object hallucination is a major obstacle to the trustworthy deployment of LVLMs.
*   **Offers a practical solution:** The proposed TruthPrInt method is designed to be applicable during inference, making it potentially deployable in real-world applications. The code is promised to be publicly available, further enhancing its practical significance.
*   **Opens new avenues for research:** The findings that universal patterns of hallucination exist in common latent subspaces opens avenues for further research on understanding and controlling LVLM behavior.

**However, the paper also has several weaknesses that need to be critically considered:**

*   **Empirical support needs to be more robust:** While the experiments show improvements over baselines, the improvement margins should be critically examined for statistical significance and practical relevance. A more thorough sensitivity analysis for key hyperparameters would strengthen the conclusions.
*   **Limited theoretical grounding:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth theoretical explanation of why the "truthful directions" are effective and why they are universal across different LVLMs. The claims about "universal patterns of hallucinations" needs further investigation and justification.
*   **Lack of ablation studies:** The ablations are mainly present for the number of traceback but not other components of the method like the specific layers chosen for intervention or different dimensions in subspaces.
*   **Clarity in writing:** The paper is a little difficult to follow in multiple places, especially regarding the description of technical components.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a potentially valuable contribution to the field of LVLM research by proposing a novel and practical approach to mitigate object hallucination. The use of internal states for per-token analysis and truthful-guided intervention is a promising direction. However, the paper suffers from certain weaknesses, including the need for more robust empirical support, limited theoretical grounding and some issues with clarity in writing. Therefore, a score of 6 is assigned.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### MuDG: Taming Multi-modal Diffusion with Gaussian Splatting for Urban Scene Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10604v1)
- **Authors**: Yingshuang Zou, Yikang Ding, Chuanrui Zhang, Jiazhe Guo, Bohan Li, Xiaoyang Lyu, Feiyang Tan, Xiaojuan Qi, Haoqian Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent breakthroughs in radiance fields have significantly advanced 3D scene reconstruction and novel view synthesis (NVS) in autonomous driving. Nevertheless, critical limitations persist: reconstruction-based methods exhibit substantial performance deterioration under significant viewpoint deviations from training trajectories, while generation-based techniques struggle with temporal coherence and precise scene controllability. To overcome these challenges, we present MuDG, an innovative framework that integrates Multi-modal Diffusion model with Gaussian Splatting (GS) for Urban Scene Reconstruction. MuDG leverages aggregated LiDAR point clouds with RGB and geometric priors to condition a multi-modal video diffusion model, synthesizing photorealistic RGB, depth, and semantic outputs for novel viewpoints. This synthesis pipeline enables feed-forward NVS without computationally intensive per-scene optimization, providing comprehensive supervision signals to refine 3DGS representations for rendering robustness enhancement under extreme viewpoint changes. Experiments on the Open Waymo Dataset demonstrate that MuDG outperforms existing methods in both reconstruction and synthesis quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "MuDG: Taming Multi-modal Diffusion with Gaussian Splatting for Urban Scene Reconstruction" introduces a new framework called MuDG for improving urban scene reconstruction and novel view synthesis (NVS). It combines a multi-modal diffusion model (MDM) with Gaussian Splatting (GS). The MDM is conditioned on sparse LiDAR point cloud projections and generates RGB, depth, and semantic videos from novel viewpoints. These synthesized videos then serve as supervisory signals to refine the GS model, leading to improved rendering robustness under extreme viewpoint changes. The key idea is to leverage the strengths of both diffusion models (for generating plausible and consistent scenes) and GS (for efficient and high-quality rendering), overcoming the limitations of each when used independently.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:* The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination and interaction of a controllable multi-modal diffusion model with Gaussian Splatting for urban scene reconstruction. While diffusion models and GS have been used separately in similar contexts, the integration where the diffusion model *supervises* the GS model is a novel contribution. The approach of using sparse LiDAR projections to condition the diffusion model for generating multi-modal outputs (RGB, depth, semantics) is also a valuable innovation. However, the core components (diffusion models, GS) are well-established. The real novelty is in the *architecture* and *training regime* that connects the two.  It's an *integration* rather than a radical new algorithm.

*Significance:* The significance stems from addressing key limitations in urban scene reconstruction and NVS, particularly for autonomous driving applications. Reconstruction-based methods often suffer from quality degradation when viewpoints deviate from the training trajectories, while generation-based methods lack temporal coherence and precise control. MuDG aims to overcome these limitations, improving both reconstruction quality and synthesis quality, as demonstrated by the results on the Waymo Open Dataset. The ability to generate high-fidelity, multi-modal data for training and simulation is highly valuable for autonomous driving research. Furthermore, the scene editing capabilities (object removal, background modification) facilitated by the controlled diffusion model add practical utility.  However, the reliance on relatively high-quality LiDAR data might limit its applicability in resource-constrained scenarios. The method's performance is also heavily dependent on the quality of the tracking algorithm used for separating dynamic and static elements.

*Strengths:*
*   Addresses a relevant and challenging problem in urban scene understanding.
*   The integrated approach of diffusion models and GS is well-motivated and effective.
*   The use of sparse LiDAR projections for conditioning the diffusion model is practical.
*   Demonstrated improvements in reconstruction and synthesis quality on a standard benchmark.
*   Scene editing capabilities offer practical benefits.

*Weaknesses:*
*   Relies on relatively high-quality LiDAR data.
*   Performance is tied to the tracking algorithm's performance.
*   The components themselves (diffusion and GS) are not fundamentally new; the novelty is in the architecture.
*   The experiments could benefit from more extensive ablation studies.

*Justification for Score:*

MuDG is a solid contribution to the field, addressing a relevant problem with a novel and effective combination of existing techniques. While the core building blocks are not groundbreaking, the architecture that combines them and, in particular, the training procedure where the diffusion model acts as a supervisor for GS is innovative. The improved results on the Waymo dataset support the effectiveness of the approach. However, the reliance on lidar, pre-existing tracking, and the iterative nature of diffusion models do limit its potential for real-time and more resource constrained applications. The ablation studies, while present, don't dig deep enough on the hyper parameters of training the GS model.

- **Classification**: cs.CV

### CoSTA$\ast$: Cost-Sensitive Toolpath Agent for Multi-turn Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10613v1)
- **Authors**: Advait Gupta, NandaKiran Velaga, Dang Nguyen, Tianyi Zhou
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image models like stable diffusion and DALLE-3 still struggle with multi-turn image editing. We decompose such a task as an agentic workflow (path) of tool use that addresses a sequence of subtasks by AI tools of varying costs. Conventional search algorithms require expensive exploration to find tool paths. While large language models (LLMs) possess prior knowledge of subtask planning, they may lack accurate estimations of capabilities and costs of tools to determine which to apply in each subtask. Can we combine the strengths of both LLMs and graph search to find cost-efficient tool paths? We propose a three-stage approach "CoSTA*" that leverages LLMs to create a subtask tree, which helps prune a graph of AI tools for the given task, and then conducts A* search on the small subgraph to find a tool path. To better balance the total cost and quality, CoSTA* combines both metrics of each tool on every subtask to guide the A* search. Each subtask's output is then evaluated by a vision-language model (VLM), where a failure will trigger an update of the tool's cost and quality on the subtask. Hence, the A* search can recover from failures quickly to explore other paths. Moreover, CoSTA* can automatically switch between modalities across subtasks for a better cost-quality trade-off. We build a novel benchmark of challenging multi-turn image editing, on which CoSTA* outperforms state-of-the-art image-editing models or agents in terms of both cost and quality, and performs versatile trade-offs upon user preference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CoSTA*: Cost-Sensitive Toolpath Agent for Multi-turn Image Editing":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoSTA*, a novel agentic mechanism for multi-turn image editing. CoSTA* addresses the challenge of performing complex image manipulations by decomposing the task into a sequence of subtasks. It leverages a large language model (LLM) for subtask planning, creating a subtask tree, and then employs an A* search algorithm on a tool dependency graph (TDG) to find the most cost-effective toolpath. CoSTA* incorporates both cost and quality metrics into the A* search heuristic and dynamically adjusts the execution cost during exploration. Moreover, the system can switch between modalities (image, text) as needed and updates the tool's cost and quality estimate based on the vision-language model (VLM) evaluation of the intermediate results. The authors create a challenging benchmark for multi-turn image editing and demonstrate that CoSTA* outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of both cost and quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The novelty of the paper lies in the combination of several elements:

*   **Hierarchical Planning:** The use of LLMs for high-level subtask planning to prune the search space is a good idea.  LLMs have demonstrated reasonable subtask planning but aren't good at assessing tool capabilities.
*   **Cost-Sensitive A* Search:** Using an A* search with cost and quality integrated into the heuristic is valuable. This allows for balancing the often-conflicting objectives. Many image editing tools exist, and selecting the right tool given a budget constraints is an important challenge.
*   **Dynamic Cost Update:** The VLM feedback mechanism to update tool cost and quality is useful for adapting to failure cases and is important in practical deployment.
*   **Modality Switching:**  Allowing the agent to dynamically select tools across image and text modalities increases the versatility of the system.

While individual components have been explored in isolation, the integration of these elements into a single agentic framework for image editing represents a worthwhile contribution.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a practically relevant problem: making multi-turn image editing more efficient and accessible. Image editing is often an iterative process, and an intelligent agent that can guide this process based on cost and quality considerations is valuable. The benchmark dataset is a valuable addition, providing a standardized way to evaluate multi-turn image editing agents. The results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods, suggesting that CoSTA* is a promising approach. The modular architecture of CoSTA*, which allows easy integration of new models/tools/datasets is another strength.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The system's complexity is also a potential weakness. The performance is heavily dependent on the accuracy of the underlying LLM, VLM, and tool benchmarks.  If these components are inaccurate, the A* search may not converge to an optimal solution. The paper does address this to some degree with dynamic cost adjustments.
*   **Limited qualitative comparison:** The qualitative results can be better expanded with detailed error analysis.
*   **Benchmark Dataset Limitations:** While the benchmark dataset is a positive contribution, its manual creation process might introduce biases. Also, while it is designed to be challenging, its reliance on synthetic edits might limit its applicability to real-world image editing scenarios, thus diminishing the results.
*   **Potential limitations:** The results show an increase in complexity for tasks. This suggests that performance can be better improved with tasks involving 8+ tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**. The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of image editing. The integration of LLMs, A* search, and dynamic cost updates is a valuable approach. The benchmark dataset is a useful resource for the community. The weaknesses of the paper involve potential dependence on the accuracy of underlying models, limitations in the benchmark datasets, and overall lack of qualitative analysis. Given the strong performance and practical value of the approach, it merits a score above average but not in the top tier due to the described limitations. The paper addresses a very practical problem and is well-written.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### R1-Onevision: Advancing Generalized Multimodal Reasoning through Cross-Modal Formalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10615v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Yang, Xiaoxuan He, Hongkun Pan, Xiyan Jiang, Yan Deng, Xingtao Yang, Haoyu Lu, Dacheng Yin, Fengyun Rao, Minfeng Zhu, Bo Zhang, Wei Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models have demonstrated remarkable reasoning capability in complex textual tasks. However, multimodal reasoning, which requires integrating visual and textual information, remains a significant challenge. Existing visual-language models often struggle to effectively analyze and reason visual content, resulting in suboptimal performance on complex reasoning tasks. Moreover, the absence of comprehensive benchmarks hinders the accurate assessment of multimodal reasoning capabilities. In this paper, we introduce R1-Onevision, a multimodal reasoning model designed to bridge the gap between visual perception and deep reasoning. To achieve this, we propose a cross-modal reasoning pipeline that transforms images into formal textural representations, enabling precise language-based reasoning. Leveraging this pipeline, we construct the R1-Onevision dataset which provides detailed, step-by-step multimodal reasoning annotations across diverse domains. We further develop the R1-Onevision model through supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning to cultivate advanced reasoning and robust generalization abilities. To comprehensively evaluate multimodal reasoning performance across different grades, we introduce R1-Onevision-Bench, a benchmark aligned with human educational stages, covering exams from junior high school to university and beyond. Experimental results show that R1-Onevision achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming models such as GPT-4o and Qwen2.5-VL on multiple challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R1-Onevision, a new multimodal reasoning model, and a corresponding dataset and benchmark (R1-Onevision-Bench). The core contribution is a cross-modal reasoning pipeline that converts images into formal textual representations, which are then processed by a language model.  The R1-Onevision dataset provides detailed, step-by-step reasoning annotations for a diverse set of visual-reasoning tasks. The model is trained using supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL).  R1-Onevision-Bench is aligned with human educational stages, providing a comprehensive evaluation of multimodal reasoning performance across various scientific domains.  Experiments show R1-Onevision achieves state-of-the-art performance, exceeding models like GPT-40 and Qwen2.5-VL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The idea of converting images to formal language representations for language model processing has been explored before, but the specific implementation within a reasoning pipeline, the generation of a detailed step-by-step multimodal reasoning dataset, and the construction of a curriculum-aware benchmark are interesting. The use of both SFT and rule-based RL is also a fairly standard, but sensible approach to fine-tuning. The novelty isn't revolutionary, but a solid combination of well-established techniques applied to a new task with new datasets.

*   **Significance:**  The work is potentially significant because it addresses the limitations of existing visual-language models in performing complex reasoning tasks, as many existing VLMs still struggle with integrating visual information in a structured manner for deeper reasoning. The dataset and benchmark will likely be a valuable resource for the community. The explicit focus on educational "grade-level" performance is also noteworthy, aligning evaluation with practical learning scenarios. A strong, open-source framework that performs well could lower the barrier to entry for those doing VQA and VLR research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive benchmark covering diverse domains and educational levels.
    *   Detailed reasoning annotations in the R1-Onevision dataset.
    *   Well-motivated design with reasonable implementation choices (cross-modal pipeline, SFT + RL).
    *   Demonstrated state-of-the-art performance on various multimodal reasoning benchmarks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The description of the "formal textual representation" generation could be more detailed. It's not entirely clear *how* GPT-4o, Grounding DINO, and EasyOCR are combined, and the specific prompt engineering plays a critical role, so better documentation/code release will boost value.
    *   The rule-based reward function is limited in scope because it only seems to penalize not including the correct answer with <think> and </think> tags. The system does not punish models for being illogical, just for not following directions with the <tags>.
    *   While results outperform many open-source alternatives and even closed-source models, the gains over carefully chosen, open-source fine-tuning strategies (like LoftQ) are not dramatically larger.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   R1-Onevision and its associated dataset and benchmark could become a valuable asset for multimodal reasoning research, providing a standardized framework for evaluating progress.
    *   The educational alignment of the benchmark could promote the development of AI systems that better support learning and education.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a tangible contribution with the dataset and benchmark. The technique of converting images into structured, formalized text for LM is a sensible, but not a highly novel approach. The performance gains, though solid, aren't game-changing. The impact on the community depends on the accessibility and quality of the released datasets, models, and code. The weaknesses are not debilitating, but they prevent the work from being a true breakthrough. A careful focus on expanding the types of RL rewards will increase the power of the reasoning capability.

### Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-tuning for Adaptive Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10617v1)
- **Authors**: Andy Zhou
- **Abstract**: Adapting large language models to multiple tasks can cause cross-skill interference, where improvements for one skill degrade another. While methods such as LoRA impose orthogonality constraints at the weight level, they do not fully address interference in hidden-state representations. We propose Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-tuning (CS-ReFT), a novel representation-based approach that learns multiple orthonormal subspace transformations, each specializing in a distinct skill, and composes them via a lightweight router. By isolating these subspace edits in the hidden state, rather than weight matrices, CS-ReFT prevents cross-task conflicts more effectively. On the AlpacaEval benchmark, applying CS-ReFT to Llama-2-7B achieves a 93.94% win rate, surpassing GPT-3.5 Turbo (86.30%) while requiring only 0.0098% of model parameters. These findings show that specialized representation edits, composed via a simple router, significantly enhance multi-task instruction following with minimal overhead.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-Tuning for Adaptive Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Compositional Subspace Representation Fine-Tuning (CS-ReFT), a novel method for adapting large language models (LLMs) to multiple tasks while mitigating cross-task interference. CS-ReFT learns multiple orthonormal subspace transformations in the hidden states of the LLM, each specializing in a distinct task.  A lightweight router then dynamically composes these subspace edits based on the input, enabling the model to handle multi-skill prompts effectively.  The method requires very few trainable parameters and achieves strong performance on the AlpacaEval benchmark, surpassing both larger models and other parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques. The key idea is to isolate task-specific edits in the hidden state, preventing them from interfering with each other and enabling compositional reasoning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in combining subspace representation editing (ReFT) with a routing mechanism for composing multiple task-specific subspaces.  While ReFT modifies hidden states, the addition of orthonormal constraints and a router to handle multiple tasks is a distinct contribution. It moves beyond a single global edit, addressing a key limitation of previous ReFT approaches. Compared to LoRA variants that impose orthogonality at the weight level, CS-ReFT applies it directly in the hidden-state space, potentially isolating skills more effectively. The use of a lightweight routing network to determine the appropriate subspace edits adds to the novelty, allowing for dynamic composition of skills.

* **Significance:** The significance stems from the improved performance and parameter efficiency of CS-ReFT. Achieving a 93.94% win rate on AlpacaEval with Llama-2-7B while using only 0.0098% of the model's parameters is a compelling result.  Outperforming GPT-3.5 Turbo on this benchmark with a much smaller and more efficient model demonstrates the potential of the approach. By showing superior performance compared to existing PEFT techniques like LoRA and other ReFT variants, the paper suggests that representation-based methods with dynamic composition can be a promising direction for multi-task LLM adaptation.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:**  The AlpacaEval results provide clear evidence of the effectiveness of CS-ReFT. The comparison against GPT-3.5 Turbo and other PEFT baselines strengthens the claim of improved performance and efficiency.
    * **Parameter Efficiency:** The very low parameter overhead (0.0098%) makes CS-ReFT highly practical for resource-constrained environments.
    * **Clear and Well-Articulated Method:** The paper provides a clear description of the CS-ReFT architecture, the subspace editing mechanism, and the routing network.
    * **Addresses a Key Limitation:** CS-ReFT directly tackles the problem of cross-task interference in multi-task adaptation, a common challenge in applying LLMs to diverse tasks.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is primarily focused on the AlpacaEval benchmark. Evaluating CS-ReFT on a broader range of tasks and datasets would further validate its effectiveness and generalizability. It's unclear how task partitioning is done in practice - is it manual or learned? More details on this aspect would be beneficial.
    * **Router Interpretability:** While the router allows for dynamic composition, the paper does not delve deeply into the interpretability of its decisions. Understanding how the router selects and composes subspaces could provide valuable insights into the model's reasoning process. The details about the routing network could also be more exhaustive.
    * **Scalability:** The paper doesn't discuss the scalability of CS-ReFT to a very large number of tasks.  While the router can potentially handle multiple subspaces, there might be limitations in terms of training complexity or interference as the number of tasks grows significantly.  Future research is required to assess the effects of scaling CS-ReFT.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence research in PEFT and multi-task LLM adaptation.  The idea of combining subspace editing with dynamic routing could inspire new approaches that leverage representation-based methods for efficient and flexible model adaptation. The strong empirical results could encourage researchers to further explore the benefits of orthonormal constraints and dynamic composition in hidden state spaces.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Given the novelty in combining representation editing with dynamic routing for multi-task learning, the significant improvement in performance and parameter efficiency demonstrated on AlpacaEval, and the potential to influence future research, I assign a score of **7.5**. While the paper has some limitations in terms of evaluation scope and interpretability, the core idea and the empirical results are strong enough to warrant a high score. The paper demonstrates a tangible advancement in PEFT and offers a promising direction for addressing cross-task interference in multi-task LLM adaptation. The use of a very small fraction of trainable parameters to outperform much larger models is a significant achievement.

### DiT-Air: Revisiting the Efficiency of Diffusion Model Architecture Design in Text to Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10618v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Chen, Rui Qian, Wenze Hu, Tsu-Jui Fu, Lezhi Li, Bowen Zhang, Alex Schwing, Wei Liu, Yinfei Yang
- **Abstract**: In this work, we empirically study Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for text-to-image generation, focusing on architectural choices, text-conditioning strategies, and training protocols. We evaluate a range of DiT-based architectures--including PixArt-style and MMDiT variants--and compare them with a standard DiT variant which directly processes concatenated text and noise inputs. Surprisingly, our findings reveal that the performance of standard DiT is comparable with those specialized models, while demonstrating superior parameter-efficiency, especially when scaled up. Leveraging the layer-wise parameter sharing strategy, we achieve a further reduction of 66% in model size compared to an MMDiT architecture, with minimal performance impact. Building on an in-depth analysis of critical components such as text encoders and Variational Auto-Encoders (VAEs), we introduce DiT-Air and DiT-Air-Lite. With supervised and reward fine-tuning, DiT-Air achieves state-of-the-art performance on GenEval and T2I CompBench, while DiT-Air-Lite remains highly competitive, surpassing most existing models despite its compact size.
- **Summary**: This paper, "DiT-Air: Revisiting the Efficiency of Diffusion Model Architecture Design in Text to Image Generation," empirically investigates Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for text-to-image generation, focusing on architectural choices, text-conditioning strategies, and training protocols. The key finding is that a standard DiT architecture, with concatenated text and noise inputs, performs comparably to more specialized models like PixArt-style and MMDiT variants, especially when scaled up, while demonstrating superior parameter efficiency. The paper introduces DiT-Air and DiT-Air-Lite, leveraging layer-wise parameter sharing for further model size reduction with minimal performance impact, and demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on GenEval and T2I CompBench. The paper also explores text encoders and VAEs, and progressively trains the models to achieve a SOTA performance.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies in its systematic and empirical exploration of DiT architectures for text-to-image generation. While the use of DiTs is not novel *per se*, the thorough analysis of various architectural components, text-conditioning mechanisms, and training strategies is a valuable contribution. The counter-intuitive finding that a simpler, standard DiT architecture performs competitively with more complex, specialized models (like MMDiT and PixArt) is significant. Furthermore, the effective parameter sharing strategies, particularly in DiT-Air-Lite, offer practical benefits in terms of model size and computational efficiency, potentially facilitating deployment on resource-constrained devices. The introduction of DiT-Air and its Lite variant contributes a new architecture with a simple concatenation-based approach for a single stream-design, differentiating it from the previously popular dual-stream approach of MMDiT. The progressive VAE training and the final stage training using supervised and reward-based finetuning also demonstrate good engineering and lead to improved performance.

However, the paper is not without its limitations. The core finding that the basic DiT structure outperforms complex models might be specific to the particular dataset and training setup used in the paper, although it provides a valuable perspective and warrants further investigation. The improvements in GenEval and CompBench are incremental rather than revolutionary, and while efficiency gains are substantial, the architectural innovation itself, mainly on the design of parameter sharing, is more evolutionary. The paper provides extensive results and well-managed experiments to validate its claims. However, there is a considerable amount of experimentation on a proprietary dataset, reducing some level of transparency and reproducibility, which has been a general issue with papers from commercial companies.

In conclusion, the paper presents a valuable empirical study with some interesting and practical findings. The paper offers an efficient architecture that achieves competitive, and even SOTA performance, despite significantly fewer parameters, enabling deployment on resource-constrained setups. The comprehensive ablations and analyses add breadth to the field.

### Siege: Autonomous Multi-Turn Jailbreaking of Large Language Models with Tree Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10619v1)
- **Authors**: Andy Zhou
- **Abstract**: We introduce Siege, a multi-turn adversarial framework that models the gradual erosion of Large Language Model (LLM) safety through a tree search perspective. Unlike single-turn jailbreaks that rely on one meticulously engineered prompt, Siege expands the conversation at each turn in a breadth-first fashion, branching out multiple adversarial prompts that exploit partial compliance from previous responses. By tracking these incremental policy leaks and re-injecting them into subsequent queries, Siege reveals how minor concessions can accumulate into fully disallowed outputs. Evaluations on the JailbreakBench dataset show that Siege achieves a 100% success rate on GPT-3.5-turbo and 97% on GPT-4 in a single multi-turn run, using fewer queries than baselines such as Crescendo or GOAT. This tree search methodology offers an in-depth view of how model safeguards degrade over successive dialogue turns, underscoring the urgency of robust multi-turn testing procedures for language models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "SIEGE: Autonomous Multi-Turn Jailbreaking of Large Language Models with Tree Search" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Siege," a novel framework designed to assess and exploit vulnerabilities in the safety mechanisms of Large Language Models (LLMs) through multi-turn interactions. Siege adopts a breadth-first tree search approach, where at each turn of a conversation, multiple adversarial prompts are generated, each branching out to explore different attack vectors. Crucially, Siege incorporates a "partial compliance" tracking mechanism. This tracks incremental policy violations by the LLM; even minor concessions or information leaks are identified, re-injected into subsequent prompts, and potentially leveraged to escalate the attack. The system aims to expose how seemingly innocuous responses can be chained together to circumvent safety safeguards. Experiments on JailbreakBench demonstrate that Siege achieves higher success rates with fewer queries compared to other multi-turn attack methods, indicating improved efficiency and effectiveness in exposing LLM vulnerabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its combination of breadth-first search with explicit tracking and leveraging of partial compliance.  Previous multi-turn jailbreaking approaches often focus on a single interaction path or lack fine-grained mechanisms to identify and exploit subtle policy violations.  The BFS approach also allows the system to explore multiple attack vectors in parallel, which is a significant improvement over single-path methods. However, aspects of the individual components, such as adversarial LLMs and tree search, are not entirely new on their own. The key contribution is the synergy of these elements in the context of LLM jailbreaking.

*   **Significance:**  The work addresses a critical gap in LLM safety evaluation: the need for robust, multi-turn adversarial testing.  Real-world attackers are likely to engage in iterative, adaptive strategies to bypass safety measures. Siege provides a framework to faithfully simulate these conditions and uncover vulnerabilities that single-turn evaluations might miss. The findings demonstrate the potential for incremental policy leaks to accumulate into severe violations, highlighting the urgency of more robust multi-turn safety validation procedures. It shows that current LLM safeguards are vulnerable to sophisticated attacks. The improved efficiency demonstrated by Siege (achieving higher success rates with fewer queries) has practical implications for scaling adversarial testing.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Approach:**  The integration of an adversarial LLM, partial compliance tracking, and tree search offers a comprehensive framework for multi-turn jailbreaking.

    *   **Demonstrated Effectiveness:** Empirical results on JailbreakBench show that Siege outperforms existing baselines (Crescendo and GOAT) in both success rate and efficiency.

    *   **Insights into LLM Vulnerabilities:** The system provides insights into how LLM safety degrades over successive dialogue turns, emphasizing the importance of addressing incremental policy leaks.

    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly explains the architecture and implementation details of Siege.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The implementation of a system with BFS and partial compliance tracking is likely complex and could present challenges for reproducibility.

    *   **Scalability:** While more efficient than baselines, the tree search approach could face scalability limitations with very long conversations or a very large number of branches.

    *   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** While JailbreakBench is a valuable dataset, further evaluations on more diverse and complex scenarios could strengthen the conclusions.

    *   **Attacker LLM Generalization:** The effectiveness may be highly dependent on the quality and diversity of the "attacker LLM's" red-teaming tactics.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust LLM safety evaluation methodologies and inspire new approaches to adversarial defense. By demonstrating the vulnerability of current safeguards to multi-turn attacks, the research could motivate the design of more sophisticated safety mechanisms that are resistant to incremental policy erosion.

**Justification for Score:**

Siege represents a significant advancement in LLM safety evaluation by offering a more realistic and effective framework for multi-turn adversarial testing. The combination of BFS with partial compliance tracking is innovative and addresses a critical gap in existing approaches. However, there are potential scalability issues and a dependence on the attack strategies initially defined for the "attacker LLM". While the components themselves aren't groundbreaking on their own, their integration and demonstrated effectiveness against standard benchmarks make this a valuable and impactful contribution.
